user
Design discussion (read-only; use a PTY if the non-PTY shell returns blank stdout). pyfin de-novo
transcript assembly. QUESTION: why do truth transcripts that HAVE full-length read support still
produce NO candidate at the GENERATION stage?

MEASURED CONTEXT (this is real, from a SGNex H9 directRNA sample, gencode v44 truth):
- pyfin's genome input is a minimap2 SPLICED genome BAM (reads aligned to the GENOME with -ax splice).
  Generation extracts each read's intron chain from its genome CIGAR (fin/candidates/intron_chains.py
  extract_intron_chain: N ops -> (donor,acceptor) EXACT genome coords) and groups reads by EXACT
  intron-chain tuple (fin/candidates/chain_cluster.py cluster_families / cluster_read_chains). A
  candidate can only '=' -match a truth transcript if some read's EXACT genome intron chain equals the
  truth's chain.
- "Full-length support" is measured from a separate TRANSCRIPTOME BAM (reads aligned to the reference
  TRANSCRIPTOME): a transcript is FL-supported if >=3 reads cover it end-to-end (start<=50bp,
  end>=L-50bp).
- Even the "keep everything" clustering (cluster_families: keeps ALL variants, NO fold, NO snap) only
  recalls 85.6% of FL>=3 truth -> ~14% (~500) FL-supported transcripts get NO exact-chain candidate.
- A genome-BAM diagnostic of the MISSED (nanocount est>=3) truth classified each missed transcript by
  its best genome read: A) an EXACT genome read exists but wasn't emitted = 1.7%; B) only WOBBLE-close
  genome reads (junctions <=6bp off, none exact) = 10%; C) only TRUNCATED sub-chain genome reads (no
  full genome chain) = 74%; D) genome reads with other structure = 12%; E) no genome reads = 1%.

So the dominant reason a FL-supported transcript yields no candidate is that its GENOME reads' chains
do NOT exactly equal the truth chain -- mostly because the reads are TRUNCATED in the genome spliced
alignment (74%) or WOBBLED (10%), even though the SAME reads look full-length against the transcriptome.

DISCUSS, reading the code (extract_intron_chain, cluster_families/cluster_read_chains, the fusion
>=250bp soft-clip exclusion in discovery.py, interval strand assignment, io_bam):

1. MECHANISM: enumerate the concrete reasons a read that is full-length in the TRANSCRIPTOME alignment
   yields a truncated / wobbled / absent GENOME intron chain: (a) minimap2 spliced genome alignment
   places junctions a few bp off (wobble) vs the transcriptome linear alignment; (b) spliced alignment
   soft-clips / drops terminal exons -> genome chain is a proper sub-chain; (c) the read's PRIMARY
   genome alignment is to a paralog/other locus (multi-map) so it never appears at this locus / this
   strand; (d) >=250bp terminal soft-clip -> fusion-excluded from generation; (e) small terminal exons
   below minimap2's detection -> dropped junction. Which of these do you judge dominant given the 74%
   truncated / 10% wobble split, and how would you confirm each from the BAMs?

2. THE HARD CONSTRAINT: the user REJECTS snapping to annotation (pyfin's differentiator: it wins
   corrupted-annotation conditions precisely because it does NOT snap). But a DATA-DRIVEN consensus
   (snap reads to their OWN modal/median junction within a wobble family; assemble a full path from
   overlapping partial reads) is NOT annotation-snap. For each of the 74% truncated and 10% wobble
   cases, is there a data-only way to synthesize the missing exact-chain candidate WITHOUT touching
   annotation? Specifically evaluate: (i) read-consensus junctions within a cluster_families family
   (does the modal junction of the pooled reads recover truth coords?); (ii) path-completion /
   intron-graph assembly of a full chain from overlapping truncated reads (the E1-I1'-E2' -> full-path
   idea); (iii) using the transcriptome alignment as a second evidence source.

3. RISK / no-double-snap: any of these consensus/assembly steps could re-introduce a soft annotation
   snap or over-merge distinct close isoforms. How to keep it evidence-only and reversible, consistent
   with "keep all variants, pool reads, let assignment decide"?

4. Recommend ONE concrete highest-leverage generation-stage change to recover these FL-supported-but-
   ungenerated transcripts, and how to measure it (structural '=' recall vs the FL>=3 denominator,
   watching precision).

Be decisive and concrete with file:line cites. Do not modify code.

DEFAULT 2 (production): on real human GENCODE it lifts precision at zero recall cost across every scenario (de novo Pr +3.8, ratios +0.8..+3.2, all corrupted-GTF +0.6..+0.9) and keeps pyfin #1 on SIRV4. 84:@click.option("--guided-junction-min-reads", default=0, show_default=True, type=int, help="(--quant-mode m2_em only, EXPERIMENT) GUIDED junction-support gate: mirror of Lever 2 for GTF-passthrough candidates — drop a guided multi-exon candidate if ANY of its junctions is spliced by fewer than N directly-observed reads (primary-read CIGAR introns, within --guided-junction-reads-tol bp). Extends the coordinate-EXACT read-support check (Lever 2 is novel-only) to GTF junctions, so a jitter-corrupted annotation junction (coords > tol from the true site) is dropped instead of surviving the coordinate-inexact M2/M1 gate. Targets the c_jitter precision collapse, and trims GTF echo in loci that DO show observed splicing (a FULLY read-sparse locus fails open — echo there is unaffected). NOT recall-safe (also drops low-coverage annotated junctions). Default 0 (OFF) pending real-data validation.")

     1	"""Intron chain extraction and read grouping for candidate discovery."""
     2	
     3	from __future__ import annotations
     4	
     5	import logging
     6	from collections import Counter, defaultdict
     7	from typing import Dict, List, Optional, Tuple
     8	
     9	from fin.candidates.dataclasses import IntronChain
    10	from fin.io.interval_manager import extract_three_prime_pos
    11	
    12	logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    13	
    14	# pysam CIGAR operation codes
    15	_CIGAR_N = 3  # skipped region (intron)
    16	
    17	
    18	def extract_intron_chain(
    19	    cigartuples: List[Tuple[int, int]], reference_start: int
    20	) -> IntronChain:
    21	    """Extract intron chain from CIGAR tuples.
    22	
    23	    Walks the CIGAR string collecting N (skip) operations as introns.
    24	
    25	    Args:
    26	        cigartuples: List of (operation, length) from pysam alignment.
    27	        reference_start: 0-based reference start position.
    28	
    29	    Returns:
    30	        IntronChain with sorted intron intervals.
    31	    """
    32	    introns = []
    33	    ref_pos = reference_start
    34	
    35	    for op, length in cigartuples:
    36	        if op == _CIGAR_N:
    37	            introns.append((ref_pos, ref_pos + length))
    38	            ref_pos += length
    39	        elif op in (0, 2, 7, 8):  # M, D, =, X consume reference
    40	            ref_pos += length
    41	        # S(4), I(1), H(5), P(6) do not consume reference
    42	
    43	    return IntronChain(introns=tuple(introns))
    44	
    45	
    46	def gtf_transcript_to_intron_chain(transcript) -> IntronChain:
    47	    """Convert a GTFTranscript to an IntronChain via gaps between sorted exons.
    48	
    49	    Args:
    50	        transcript: GTFTranscript instance with .exons attribute.
    51	
    52	    Returns:
    53	        IntronChain derived from gaps between exons.
    54	    """
    55	    exons = sorted(transcript.exons, key=lambda e: e[0])
    56	    introns = []
    57	    for i in range(len(exons) - 1):
    58	        intron_start = exons[i][1]
    59	        intron_end = exons[i + 1][0]
    60	        if intron_end > intron_start:
    61	            introns.append((intron_start, intron_end))
    62	    return IntronChain(introns=tuple(introns))
    63	
    64	
    65	def _cluster_positions(positions: List[int], gap: int) -> List[List[int]]:
    66	    """Greedy 1D clustering: positions within ``gap`` bp join the active cluster.
    67	
    68	    Args:
    69	        positions: Unsorted list of integer coordinates. Duplicates are kept.
    70	        gap: Maximum allowed distance from the previous (sorted) position to
    71	            stay in the same cluster.
    72	
    73	    Returns:
    74	        List of clusters; each cluster is a list of positions in sorted order
    75	        (duplicates preserved so callers can compute support counts).
    76	    """
    77	    if not positions:
    78	        return []
    79	    sorted_pos = sorted(positions)
    80	    clusters: List[List[int]] = [[sorted_pos[0]]]
    81	    for p in sorted_pos[1:]:
    82	        if p - clusters[-1][-1] <= gap:
    83	            clusters[-1].append(p)
    84	        else:
    85	            clusters.append([p])
    86	    return clusters
    87	
    88	
    89	def _has_canonical_motif(
    90	    pos: int,
    91	    kind: str,
    92	    strand: str,
    93	    genome_seq: str,
    94	) -> bool:
    95	    """Check whether a donor/acceptor position carries the canonical splice motif.
    96	
    97	    Splice site conventions (0-based, half-open intron [s, e)):
    98	        + strand: donor=GT at genome[s:s+2], acceptor=AG at genome[e-2:e].
    99	        - strand: donor=AC at genome[e-2:e], acceptor=CT at genome[s:s+2].
   100	
   101	    Args:
   102	        pos: For a donor on + strand or acceptor on - strand, this is the
   103	            intron start ``s``. For an acceptor on + strand or donor on -
   104	            strand, this is the intron end ``e``.
   105	        kind: "donor" or "acceptor".
   106	        strand: "+" or "-".
   107	        genome_seq: Full chromosome sequence string.
   108	
   109	    Returns:
   110	        True if the 2 bp motif at the expected offset matches.
   111	    """
   112	    if not genome_seq:
   113	        return False
   114	    n = len(genome_seq)
   115	    if strand == "+":
   116	        if kind == "donor":
   117	            if pos < 0 or pos + 2 > n:
   118	                return False
   119	            return genome_seq[pos:pos + 2].upper() == "GT"
   120	        # acceptor on + strand: pos is intron end e, motif at [e-2, e)
   121	        if pos - 2 < 0 or pos > n:
   122	            return False
   123	        return genome_seq[pos - 2:pos].upper() == "AG"
   124	    # '-' strand
   125	    if kind == "donor":
   126	        # donor on - strand: pos is intron end e, motif at [e-2, e) == "AC"
   127	        if pos - 2 < 0 or pos > n:
   128	            return False
   129	        return genome_seq[pos - 2:pos].upper() == "AC"
   130	    # acceptor on - strand: pos is intron start s, motif at [s, s+2) == "CT"
   131	    if pos < 0 or pos + 2 > n:
   132	        return False
   133	    return genome_seq[pos:pos + 2].upper() == "CT"
   134	
   135	
   136	def _pick_consensus(
   137	    cluster_positions: List[int],
   138	    kind: str,
   139	    strand: str,
   140	    genome_seq: str,
   141	) -> int:
   142	    """Pick a consensus position for a cluster of donor or acceptor sites.
   143	
   144	    Priority order (per the junction-snap algorithm):
   145	      1. Most-supported position whose flanking motif is canonical.
   146	      2. Most-supported position overall (mode).
   147	      3. Median (tie-break — applied implicitly because Counter.most_common is
   148	         stable; we additionally pick the median when ties remain).
   149	
   150	    Args:
   151	        cluster_positions: Positions inside one cluster (duplicates allowed,
   152	            their counts encode read support).
   153	        kind: "donor" or "acceptor".
   154	        strand: "+" or "-".
   155	        genome_seq: Full chromosome sequence string.
   156	
   157	    Returns:
   158	        Chosen consensus coordinate.
   159	    """
   160	    counts = Counter(cluster_positions)
   161	    # Group positions by their support count, descending.
   162	    ordered = sorted(counts.items(), key=lambda kv: (-kv[1], kv[0]))
   163	
   164	    # 1. Highest-support canonical-motif position.
   165	    top_support = ordered[0][1]
   166	    top_tier = [p for p, c in ordered if c == top_support]
   167	    canonical_top = [p for p in top_tier if _has_canonical_motif(p, kind, strand, genome_seq)]
   168	    if canonical_top:
   169	        return min(canonical_top, key=lambda p: (abs(p - _median(top_tier)), p))
   170	
   171	    # Look further down support tiers for any canonical motif before falling
   172	    # back to mode/median; this lets a slightly-less-supported canonical site
   173	    # beat noisy non-canonical positions at higher support.
   174	    for p, _c in ordered:
   175	        if _has_canonical_motif(p, kind, strand, genome_seq):
   176	            # Only override the mode if its support is at least half the mode's.
   177	            if counts[p] * 2 >= top_support:
   178	                return p
   179	            break
   180	
   181	    # 2. Mode (most-supported overall). Among ties, take the median.
   182	    if len(top_tier) == 1:
   183	        return top_tier[0]
   184	    return _median(top_tier)
   185	
   186	
   187	def _median(values: List[int]) -> int:
   188	    """Return the lower median of ``values`` (matches existing 3'-end logic)."""
   189	    s = sorted(values)
   190	    return s[len(s) // 2]
   191	
   192	
   193	def snap_junctions(
   194	    read_chains: List[Tuple[Dict, IntronChain]],
   195	    genome_seq: str,
   196	    strand: str,
   197	    snap_bp: int = 6,
   198	    min_support: int = 2,
   199	) -> List[Tuple[Dict, IntronChain]]:
   200	    """Snap noisy splice-site coordinates onto per-cluster consensus positions.
   201	
   202	    Algorithm:
   203	      1. Pool every read's donor (intron start) and acceptor (intron end)
   204	         positions independently.
   205	      2. Greedy 1D-cluster each pool with ``snap_bp`` as the join gap.
   206	      3. Pick a consensus per cluster, preferring canonical splice motifs
   207	         (see :func:`_pick_consensus`).
   208	      4. Rewrite each read's chain to the consensus coordinates. Clusters
   209	         with fewer than ``min_support`` reads keep their original positions
   210	         to avoid over-snapping rare junctions.
   211	      5. Drop any rewritten intron that becomes degenerate (``s >= e``).
   212	
   213	    The function does not mutate the input read dicts; it returns a new list
   214	    of ``(read_dict, IntronChain)`` tuples with replaced (or unchanged) chains.
   215	
   216	    Args:
   217	        read_chains: Per-read ``(read_dict, IntronChain)`` pairs.
   218	        genome_seq: Full chromosome sequence string used for motif lookup.
   219	        strand: "+" or "-".
   220	        snap_bp: Maximum within-cluster gap. Must be > 0 to do any work.
   221	        min_support: Minimum number of reads in a cluster before it is
   222	            snapped to its consensus.
   223	
   224	    Returns:
   225	        A list parallel to ``read_chains`` with (possibly) rewritten chains.
   226	    """
   227	    if snap_bp <= 0 or not read_chains:
   228	        return list(read_chains)
   229	
   230	    donors: List[int] = []
   231	    acceptors: List[int] = []
   232	    for _rd, chain in read_chains:
   233	        for s, e in chain.introns:
   234	            donor_pos = s if strand == "+" else e
   235	            acceptor_pos = e if strand == "+" else s
   236	            donors.append(donor_pos)
   237	            acceptors.append(acceptor_pos)
   238	
   239	    donor_clusters = _cluster_positions(donors, snap_bp)
   240	    acceptor_clusters = _cluster_positions(acceptors, snap_bp)
   241	
   242	    donor_map: Dict[int, int] = {}
   243	    for cluster in donor_clusters:
   244	        if len(cluster) < min_support:
   245	            for p in cluster:
   246	                donor_map[p] = p
   247	            continue
   248	        consensus = _pick_consensus(cluster, "donor", strand, genome_seq)
   249	        for p in cluster:
   250	            donor_map[p] = consensus
   251	
   252	    acceptor_map: Dict[int, int] = {}
   253	    for cluster in acceptor_clusters:
   254	        if len(cluster) < min_support:
   255	            for p in cluster:
   256	                acceptor_map[p] = p
   257	            continue
   258	        consensus = _pick_consensus(cluster, "acceptor", strand, genome_seq)
   259	        for p in cluster:
   260	            acceptor_map[p] = consensus
   261	
   262	    snapped: List[Tuple[Dict, IntronChain]] = []
   263	    for rd, chain in read_chains:
   264	        new_introns: List[Tuple[int, int]] = []
   265	        for s, e in chain.introns:
   266	            if strand == "+":
   267	                new_s = donor_map.get(s, s)
   268	                new_e = acceptor_map.get(e, e)
   269	            else:
   270	                new_s = acceptor_map.get(s, s)
   271	                new_e = donor_map.get(e, e)
   272	            if new_e > new_s:
   273	                new_introns.append((new_s, new_e))
   274	            # else: degenerate after snap, drop the intron
   275	        snapped.append((rd, IntronChain(introns=tuple(new_introns))))
   276	
   277	    return snapped
   278	
   279	
   280	def _find_canonical_motif_positions(
   281	    genome_seq: str,
   282	    center: int,
   283	    kind: str,
   284	    strand: str,
   285	    search_bp: int,
   286	) -> List[int]:
   287	    """All positions in [center-search_bp, center+search_bp] whose 2 bp motif
   288	    matches the canonical donor (GT/AC) or acceptor (AG/CT) for ``strand``.
   289	
   290	    The motif convention is the same as :func:`_has_canonical_motif`.
   291	    """
   292	    out: List[int] = []
   293	    for delta in range(-search_bp, search_bp + 1):
   294	        pos = center + delta
   295	        if _has_canonical_motif(pos, kind, strand, genome_seq):
   296	            out.append(pos)
   297	    return out
   298	
   299	
   300	def expand_canonical_chain_alternatives(
   301	    read_chains: List[Tuple[Dict, IntronChain]],
   302	    genome_seq: str,
   303	    strand: str,
   304	    search_bp: int = 4,
   305	    max_chains_per_read: int = 16,
   306	) -> Dict[str, List[IntronChain]]:
   307	    """For each read, build the set of intron-chain alternatives obtained by
   308	    moving each junction's donor and/or acceptor onto any canonical GT-AG
   309	    motif within ``search_bp`` of the read's CIGAR-derived position.
   310	
   311	    The original chain is always retained (so reads whose alignments already
   312	    sit on the true position do not lose strict matches). Junctions without
   313	    any nearby canonical motif also fall back to the original position.
   314	
   315	    The total number of chains per read is bounded by ``max_chains_per_read``:
   316	    if the per-intron Cartesian product would exceed the cap, the read keeps
   317	    only its original chain (no expansion).
   318	
   319	    Args:
   320	        read_chains: Per-read ``(read_dict, IntronChain)`` pairs.
   321	        genome_seq: Full chromosome sequence string for motif lookup.
   322	        strand: "+" or "-".
   323	        search_bp: Window radius (bp) around each junction position.
   324	        max_chains_per_read: Hard cap on the per-read alternative count.
   325	
   326	    Returns:
   327	        Mapping ``query_name -> [IntronChain, ...]`` (each list non-empty;
   328	        contains at least the original chain).
   329	    """
   330	    import itertools
   331	
   332	    out: Dict[str, List[IntronChain]] = {}
   333	    if search_bp <= 0:
   334	        return out
   335	
   336	    for rd, chain in read_chains:
   337	        qname = rd.get("query_name")
   338	        if qname is None:
   339	            continue
   340	        if not chain.introns:
   341	            out[qname] = [chain]
   342	            continue
   343	
   344	        per_intron_alts: List[List[Tuple[int, int]]] = []
   345	        for s, e in chain.introns:
   346	            donor_center = s if strand == "+" else e
   347	            acceptor_center = e if strand == "+" else s
   348	
   349	            donor_alts = _find_canonical_motif_positions(
   350	                genome_seq, donor_center, "donor", strand, search_bp
   351	            )
   352	            acceptor_alts = _find_canonical_motif_positions(
   353	                genome_seq, acceptor_center, "acceptor", strand, search_bp
   354	            )
   355	            # Always include the original positions so reads whose CIGAR is
   356	            # already correct do not lose their strict match.
   357	            if donor_center not in donor_alts:
   358	                donor_alts.append(donor_center)
   359	            if acceptor_center not in acceptor_alts:
   360	                acceptor_alts.append(acceptor_center)
   361	
   362	            intron_alts: List[Tuple[int, int]] = []
   363	            seen = set()
   364	            for d in donor_alts:
   365	                for a in acceptor_alts:
   366	                    ns, ne = (d, a) if strand == "+" else (a, d)
   367	                    if ne > ns and (ns, ne) not in seen:
   368	                        seen.add((ns, ne))
   369	                        intron_alts.append((ns, ne))
   370	            if not intron_alts:
   371	                intron_alts = [(s, e)]
   372	            per_intron_alts.append(intron_alts)
   373	
   374	        # Quick size check before exploding the Cartesian product.
   375	        total = 1
   376	        for ia in per_intron_alts:
   377	            total *= len(ia)
   378	            if total > max_chains_per_read:
   379	                break
   380	        if total > max_chains_per_read:
   381	            out[qname] = [chain]
   382	            continue
   383	
   384	        chains_for_read: List[IntronChain] = []
   385	        seen_chains = set()
   386	        for combo in itertools.product(*per_intron_alts):
   387	            key = tuple(combo)
   388	            if key in seen_chains:
   389	                continue
   390	            seen_chains.add(key)
   391	            chains_for_read.append(IntronChain(introns=key))
   392	        if not chains_for_read:
   393	            chains_for_read = [chain]
   394	        out[qname] = chains_for_read
   395	
   396	    return out
   397	
   398	
   399	def expand_canonical_chain_alternatives_v2(
   400	    read_chains: List[Tuple[Dict, IntronChain]],
   401	    genome_seq: str,
   402	    strand: str,
   403	    motif_set,
   404	    search_bp: int = 4,
   405	    max_chains_per_read: int = 16,
   406	) -> Dict[str, List[IntronChain]]:
   407	    """Motif-aware canonical chain expansion (Stage C; shared-module backed).
   408	
   409	    Same role as :func:`expand_canonical_chain_alternatives` but fixes the
   410	    legacy donor/acceptor-independent Cartesian bug: per-junction alternatives
   411	    are enumerated as PAIRED (s', e') candidates via
   412	    :func:`fin.candidates.canonical.canonical_intron_alts`, so only valid
   413	    (donor, acceptor) motif pairings from ``motif_set`` are produced. The same
   414	    ``motif_set`` is shared with the canonical FILTER ("search what you filter").
   415	
   416	    The original chain is ALWAYS retained (reads already on the true junction
   417	    keep their strict match). Per-read chain count is bounded by
   418	    ``max_chains_per_read``: if the per-intron Cartesian product would exceed the
   419	    cap, the read keeps only its original chain (no expansion).
   420	
   421	    Args:
   422	        read_chains: Per-read ``(read_dict, IntronChain)`` pairs.
   423	        genome_seq: Full chromosome sequence string for motif lookup.
   424	        strand: "+" or "-".
   425	        motif_set: Frozenset of canonical (donor, acceptor) pairs to accept.
   426	        search_bp: Window radius (bp) around each junction. 0 disables.
   427	        max_chains_per_read: Hard cap on the per-read alternative count.
   428	
   429	    Returns:
   430	        Mapping ``query_name -> [IntronChain, ...]`` (each list non-empty;
   431	        contains at least the original chain).
   432	    """
   433	    import itertools
   434	
   435	    from fin.candidates.canonical import canonical_intron_alts
   436	
   437	    out: Dict[str, List[IntronChain]] = {}
   438	    if search_bp <= 0 or not genome_seq:
   439	        return out
   440	
   441	    for rd, chain in read_chains:
   442	        qname = rd.get("query_name")
   443	        if qname is None:
   444	            continue
   445	        if not chain.introns:
   446	            out[qname] = [chain]
   447	            continue
   448	
   449	        per_intron_alts: List[List[Tuple[int, int]]] = []
   450	        for s, e in chain.introns:
   451	            # Always keep the original junction first so reads already on the
   452	            # true (possibly non-canonical) position never lose their match.
   453	            alts: List[Tuple[int, int]] = [(s, e)]
   454	            for ns, ne in canonical_intron_alts(
   455	                s, e, strand, genome_seq, motif_set, search_bp
   456	            ):
   457	                if (ns, ne) != (s, e):
   458	                    alts.append((ns, ne))
   459	            per_intron_alts.append(alts)
   460	
   461	        # Quick size check before exploding the Cartesian product.
   462	        total = 1
   463	        for ia in per_intron_alts:
   464	            total *= len(ia)
   465	            if total > max_chains_per_read:
   466	                break
   467	        if total > max_chains_per_read:
   468	            out[qname] = [chain]
   469	            continue
   470	
   471	        chains_for_read: List[IntronChain] = []
   472	        seen_chains = set()
   473	        for combo in itertools.product(*per_intron_alts):
   474	            key = tuple(combo)
   475	            if key in seen_chains:
   476	                continue
   477	            seen_chains.add(key)
   478	            chains_for_read.append(IntronChain(introns=key))
   479	        if not chains_for_read:
   480	            chains_for_read = [chain]
   481	        out[qname] = chains_for_read
   482	
   483	    return out
   484	
   485	
   486	def group_reads_by_three_prime_and_intron_chain(
   487	    read_dicts: List[Dict],
   488	    strand: str,
   489	    threshold: int = 24,
   490	    chain_override: Optional[Dict[str, IntronChain]] = None,
   491	    multi_chain_override: Optional[Dict[str, List[IntronChain]]] = None,
   492	) -> Dict[Tuple[int, IntronChain], List[Dict]]:
   493	    """Group reads by clustered 3' end position and intron chain.
   494	
   495	    1. Extract 3' position and intron chain per read.
   496	    2. Sort by 3' position.
   497	    3. Cluster 3' positions within *threshold* bp.
   498	    4. Sub-group each cluster by intron chain.
   499	    5. Use consensus (median) 3' position per cluster.
   500	
   501	    Args:
   502	        read_dicts: List of alignment dicts from BamReader.alignment_to_dict().
   503	        strand: '+' or '-'.
   504	        threshold: Maximum distance between 3' ends in a cluster.
   505	        chain_override: Optional mapping ``query_name -> IntronChain`` that
   506	            replaces the CIGAR-derived chain (used for junction-snap output).
   507	        multi_chain_override: Optional mapping ``query_name -> [IntronChain, ...]``
   508	            that produces multiple grouping entries per read (used for the
   509	            canonical-motif alternative expansion). Takes precedence over
   510	            ``chain_override`` when both are provided for the same read.
   511	
   512	    Returns:
   513	        Dict mapping (consensus_3prime, IntronChain) -> list of read dicts.
   514	    """
   515	    # Annotate each read with its 3' pos and intron chain
   516	    annotated: List[Tuple[int, IntronChain, Dict]] = []
   517	    for rd in read_dicts:
   518	        three_prime = extract_three_prime_pos(rd)
   519	        if three_prime is None:
   520	            continue
   521	        cigar = rd.get("cigartuples")
   522	        ref_start = rd.get("reference_start")
   523	        if cigar is None or ref_start is None:
   524	            continue
   525	        qname = rd.get("query_name")
   526	        chains_for_this_read: Optional[List[IntronChain]] = None
   527	        if multi_chain_override is not None and qname is not None \
   528	                and qname in multi_chain_override:
   529	            chains_for_this_read = multi_chain_override[qname]
   530	        elif chain_override is not None and qname is not None \
   531	                and qname in chain_override:
   532	            chains_for_this_read = [chain_override[qname]]
   533	        if chains_for_this_read is None:
   534	            chains_for_this_read = [extract_intron_chain(cigar, ref_start)]
   535	        for chain in chains_for_this_read:
   536	            annotated.append((three_prime, chain, rd))
   537	
   538	    if not annotated:
   539	        return {}
   540	
   541	    # Sort by 3' position
   542	    annotated.sort(key=lambda x: x[0])
   543	
   544	    # Cluster by 3' proximity
   545	    clusters: List[List[Tuple[int, IntronChain, Dict]]] = []
   546	    current_cluster: List[Tuple[int, IntronChain, Dict]] = [annotated[0]]
   547	
   548	    for item in annotated[1:]:
   549	        if item[0] - current_cluster[-1][0] <= threshold:
   550	            current_cluster.append(item)
   551	        else:
   552	            clusters.append(current_cluster)
   553	            current_cluster = [item]
   554	    clusters.append(current_cluster)
   555	
   556	    # Sub-group each cluster by intron chain, compute consensus 3'
   557	    groups: Dict[Tuple[int, IntronChain], List[Dict]] = {}
   558	    for cluster in clusters:
   559	        # Consensus 3' = median of cluster positions
   560	        positions = [x[0] for x in cluster]
   561	        consensus_3prime = sorted(positions)[len(positions) // 2]
   562	
   563	        by_chain: Dict[IntronChain, List[Dict]] = defaultdict(list)
   564	        for _, chain, rd in cluster:
   565	            by_chain[chain].append(rd)
   566	
   567	        for chain, reads in by_chain.items():
   568	            groups[(consensus_3prime, chain)] = reads
   569	
   570	    return groups
   571	
   572	
   573	def pick_representative_read(read_dicts: List[Dict]) -> Dict:
   574	    """Pick the longest read as the representative for a group.
   575	
   576	    Args:
   577	        read_dicts: List of alignment dicts.
   578	
   579	    Returns:
   580	        The read dict with the largest query_length.
   581	    """
   582	    return max(read_dicts, key=lambda rd: rd.get("query_length", 0))
===== fin/candidates/chain_cluster.py =====
     1	"""Generation-side intron-chain clustering.
     2	
     3	Cluster-first candidate generation. Pool all reads, group by their EXACT (raw,
     4	un-snapped) intron chain, then:
     5	
     6	  1. COLLAPSE exact sub-chains  -- a chain that is a strict contiguous sub-chain of a
     7	     longer chain with IDENTICAL coordinates folds into it (reads pooled, chain gone).
     8	  2. CLUSTER the survivors      -- union-find groups the remaining distinct chains into
     9	     one cluster when related by any of:
    10	       * WOBBLE      : same intron count, every junction within ``wobble_bp``,
    11	       * CASSETTE    : K vs K-1 chains differing by one small (< ``cassette_max_exon_bp``)
    12	                       skipped exon (minimap2's small-exon-skip artefact),
    13	       * CONTAINMENT : a shorter chain that is a contiguous sub-chain of a longer one
    14	                       within ``wobble_bp`` per junction (the non-exact leftovers that
    15	                       step 1 did not fold).
    16	
    17	A cluster KEEPS all its member candidates (distinct chains + pooled reads); only exact
    18	sub-chains disappear. NO consensus snapping (coordinates are never rewritten). The
    19	cluster is the downstream unit: reads attach to a cluster instead of splitting across
    20	near-duplicates, and which members are genuine isoforms is decided later by the
    21	per-cluster computation. The caller runs with canonical_search_bp = 0 (no shadows).
    22	
    23	Mono-exon chains never join a structural cluster; each unique mono chain is a singleton.
    24	"""
    25	from __future__ import annotations
    26	
    27	from dataclasses import dataclass, field
    28	from typing import Dict, List, Optional, Set, Tuple
    29	
    30	from fin.candidates.dataclasses import IntronChain
    31	
    32	Chain = Tuple[Tuple[int, int], ...]
    33	
    34	
    35	@dataclass
    36	class GenCandidate:
    37	    """One candidate inside a cluster: a distinct intron chain + its pooled reads.
    38	
    39	    ``folded`` holds the SHADOW sub-chains folded into this candidate (each an exact
    40	    contiguous sub-chain, with its OWN reads). They are dormant provenance for the
    41	    downstream short-isoform recovery step: their truncation boundary is a proposed
    42	    TSS/polyA site and their reads are the direct endpoint evidence. ``read_ids``
    43	    already INCLUDES every shadow's reads (pooled), so shadows never add read mass."""
    44	    chain: IntronChain
    45	    read_ids: Set[str] = field(default_factory=set)
    46	    folded: List["GenCandidate"] = field(default_factory=list)
    47	
    48	    @property
    49	    def num_introns(self) -> int:
    50	        return len(self.chain.introns)
    51	
    52	
    53	@dataclass
    54	class ChainCluster:
    55	    """A structural family of candidate chains sharing their reads' assignment.
    56	
    57	    ``members`` are the distinct surviving candidates (exact sub-chains already folded
    58	    away). ``read_ids`` is the union of all members' reads. ``representative`` is the
    59	    longest member (convenience; NOT a snapped consensus -- original coordinates)."""
    60	    members: List[GenCandidate]
    61	    read_ids: Set[str] = field(default_factory=set)
    62	
    63	    @property
    64	    def representative(self) -> GenCandidate:
    65	        return max(self.members,
    66	                   key=lambda m: (m.num_introns, len(m.read_ids), m.chain.introns))
    67	
    68	
    69	@dataclass
    70	class ChainFamily:
    71	    """A clustering-only family of related MULTI-EXON structural variants.
    72	
    73	    ``variants`` are the distinct exact intron chains (structures) grouped together by
    74	    wobble / cassette / containment -- NO coordinates are merged or snapped, and every
    75	    variant is KEPT (no fold). ``read_pool`` is the union of all reads whose exact chain is
    76	    in this family: once a family forms, reads belong to the FAMILY, not to an individual
    77	    variant -- which variant a read truly supports is re-decided later, by evidence, in the
    78	    per-cluster assignment. ``variant_reads`` is the per-variant discovery provenance (which
    79	    exact chain each read produced), kept only for choosing a representative and for the
    80	    later path-completion step; it is NOT the final read ownership.
    81	
    82	    ``gtf_members`` are annotation transcripts attached to this family as source-tagged, ZERO-read
    83	    structural hypotheses: each is ``(gtf_id, intron_chain)``. They carry NO reads (never in
    84	    ``read_pool`` / ``variant_reads``) and get read mass ONLY if their exact model wins the downstream
    85	    evidence assignment -- annotation is another competitor, never a snap target or a read-pool source.
    86	    A family with empty ``variants`` but non-empty ``gtf_members`` is a GTF-only family (an annotated
    87	    transcript that matched no read-derived structure; kept so it survives to the abundance filters).
    88	    """
    89	    variants: List[Chain]
    90	    read_pool: Set[str] = field(default_factory=set)
    91	    variant_reads: Dict[Chain, Set[str]] = field(default_factory=dict)
    92	    gtf_members: List[Tuple[str, Chain]] = field(default_factory=list)
    93	
    94	    @property
    95	    def representative(self) -> Optional[Chain]:
    96	        """Longest read variant (tie-broken by discovery support then coords), or None for a
    97	        GTF-only family.
    98	
    99	        A convenience pick, NOT a snapped consensus -- coordinates are never rewritten.
   100	        """
   101	        if not self.variants:
   102	            return None
   103	        return max(self.variants,
   104	                   key=lambda c: (len(c), len(self.variant_reads.get(c, ())), c))
   105	
   106	
   107	@dataclass
   108	class ClusterResult:
   109	    """Output of the clustering-only step: multi-exon families + the interval mono bucket.
   110	
   111	    ``mono_reads`` is the holding bucket of every single-exon (chain-less) read in the
   112	    interval -- deliberately UNPROCESSED here (not folded, not locus-split). Whether each is
   113	    a genuine intronless transcript or a fragment of a surviving multi-exon candidate is a
   114	    later decision, made after multi-exon resolution (see the clustering redesign).
   115	    """
   116	    families: List[ChainFamily]
   117	    mono_reads: Set[str] = field(default_factory=set)
   118	
   119	
   120	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   121	# chain-relation predicates (single interval/strand: no chrom/strand bucketing)
   122	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   123	def _exact_subchain(short: Chain, long_: Chain) -> bool:
   124	    """``short`` is a strict EXACT (identical coords) contiguous sub-chain of ``long_``."""
   125	    n, m = len(long_), len(short)
   126	    if m == 0 or m >= n:
   127	        return False
   128	    return any(long_[off:off + m] == short for off in range(n - m + 1))
   129	
   130	
   131	def _subchain_offset(short: Chain, long_: Chain) -> int:
   132	    """Offset of the first exact ``short`` occurrence in ``long_`` (-1 if none). The
   133	    introns of ``long_`` outside ``[off, off+len(short))`` are the container's EXTRA
   134	    introns relative to ``short`` -- positions where ``short`` splices differently."""
   135	    n, m = len(long_), len(short)
   136	    if m == 0 or m >= n:
   137	        return -1
   138	    for off in range(n - m + 1):
   139	        if long_[off:off + m] == short:
   140	            return off
   141	    return -1
   142	
   143	
   144	def _wobble(a: Chain, b: Chain, bp: int) -> bool:
   145	    """Same intron count, every donor/acceptor within ``bp``."""
   146	    if len(a) != len(b):
   147	        return False
   148	    return all(abs(s1 - s2) <= bp and abs(e1 - e2) <= bp
   149	               for (s1, e1), (s2, e2) in zip(a, b))
   150	
   151	
   152	def _contains(short: Chain, long_: Chain, bp: int) -> bool:
   153	    """``short`` is a strictly-shorter contiguous sub-chain of ``long_`` within ``bp``."""
   154	    n, m = len(long_), len(short)
   155	    if m == 0 or m >= n:
   156	        return False
   157	    for off in range(n - m + 1):
   158	        if all(abs(short[k][0] - long_[off + k][0]) <= bp and
   159	               abs(short[k][1] - long_[off + k][1]) <= bp for k in range(m)):
   160	            return True
   161	    return False
   162	
   163	
   164	def _cassette(a: Chain, b: Chain, bp: int, max_exon_bp: int) -> bool:
   165	    """``a`` (K introns) is a cassette-skip sibling of ``b`` (K-1 introns): at one
   166	    position ``i`` the two introns ``a[i], a[i+1]`` replace ``b[i]``'s span (outer
   167	    donor/acceptor within bp), the skipped exon between them is < ``max_exon_bp``, and
   168	    every other intron matches within bp."""
   169	    if max_exon_bp <= 0 or len(a) != len(b) + 1:
   170	        return False
   171	    for i in range(len(a) - 1):
   172	        if any(abs(a[k][0] - b[k][0]) > bp or abs(a[k][1] - b[k][1]) > bp
   173	               for k in range(i)):
   174	            continue
   175	        if abs(a[i][0] - b[i][0]) > bp or abs(a[i + 1][1] - b[i][1]) > bp:
   176	            continue
   177	        rest = len(b) - i - 1
   178	        if any(abs(a[i + 2 + k][0] - b[i + 1 + k][0]) > bp or
   179	               abs(a[i + 2 + k][1] - b[i + 1 + k][1]) > bp for k in range(rest)):
   180	            continue
   181	        cas = a[i + 1][0] - a[i][1]
   182	        if 0 < cas < max_exon_bp:
   183	            return True
   184	    return False
   185	
   186	
   187	def _related(a: Chain, b: Chain, wobble_bp: int, cassette_max_exon_bp: int) -> bool:
   188	    """True iff ``a`` and ``b`` should share a cluster (wobble / cassette / containment)."""
   189	    if _wobble(a, b, wobble_bp):
   190	        return True
   191	    lo, hi = (a, b) if len(a) < len(b) else (b, a)
   192	    if len(lo) < len(hi):
   193	        if _cassette(hi, lo, wobble_bp, cassette_max_exon_bp):
   194	            return True
   195	        if _contains(lo, hi, wobble_bp):
   196	            return True
   197	    return False
   198	
   199	
   200	def _monoexon_in_exon(s: int, e: int, chain: Chain, lo: int, hi: int) -> bool:
   201	    """``[s, e)`` (a single-exon read) lies wholly inside ONE exon of a multi-exon
   202	    candidate whose exonic footprint is ``[lo, hi)`` and whose introns are ``chain``
   203	    (each ``[d, a)`` half-open). True iff the read is within the footprint AND overlaps
   204	    no intron -- i.e. it is a fragment of that candidate's exonic sequence, not an
   205	    intronic feature (a different gene) and not something that crosses a junction."""
   206	    if s < lo or e > hi:
   207	        return False
   208	    for d, a in chain:                       # overlap of [s,e) and intron [d,a)
   209	        if s < a and d < e:
   210	            return False
   211	    return True
   212	
   213	
   214	def cluster_read_chains(
   215	    read_chains: List[Tuple[Dict, IntronChain]],
   216	    *,
   217	    wobble_bp: int = 6,
   218	    cassette_max_exon_bp: int = 70,
   219	    fold_monoexon_contained: bool = False,
   220	    fold_span_guard: bool = False,
   221	) -> List[ChainCluster]:
   222	    """Build generation-side clusters from per-read chains (NO snap, 3' ignored).
   223	
   224	    Args:
   225	        read_chains: per-read ``(read_dict, IntronChain)`` (read_dict needs
   226	            "query_name"; CIGAR-derived chains, used as-is).
   227	        wobble_bp: per-junction tolerance for wobble / cassette / containment joins.
   228	        cassette_max_exon_bp: max skipped-exon length for a cassette join (0 off).
   229	        fold_monoexon_contained: when True, a single-exon (chain-less) read whose
   230	            aligned span lies wholly inside one exon of a multi-exon candidate is folded
   231	            into that candidate (its read joins the candidate's support) instead of
   232	            emitting a standalone mono candidate. This absorbs 5'/3' degradation
   233	            fragments of a spliced transcript. Reads inside an INTRON (a different gene)
   234	            or not contained in any multi-exon candidate stay as mono candidates. Off by
   235	            default (byte-identical legacy behaviour).
   236	        fold_span_guard: when True, a read only folds into an exact-sub-chain container
   237	            if its aligned span does NOT run exonically across one of the container's
   238	            EXTRA introns (introns present in the container but absent from the read's
   239	            chain). A read that spans such an intron contradicts it (retained-intron or
   240	            alternative isoform) and is kept as its own candidate instead of being
   241	            absorbed. Off by default (byte-identical legacy behaviour).
   242	
   243	    Returns:
   244	        List of :class:`ChainCluster`. EVERY exact contiguous sub-chain is folded
   245	        (unconditionally, no read-support guard) into its longest exact container --
   246	        generation does pure structural collapse and does NOT decide whether a folded
   247	        sub-chain is a real short isoform; that judgement is a SEPARATE downstream
   248	        recovery step (e.g. read-end pileup), not done here. The survivors are unioned
   249	        into clusters by wobble/cassette/containment; each unique mono chain is its own
   250	        singleton cluster.
   251	    """
   252	    # 1. group reads by EXACT chain (no 3', no snap).
   253	    by_chain: Dict[Chain, Set[str]] = {}
   254	    for rd, chain in read_chains:
   255	        rid = rd.get("query_name")
   256	        if rid is None:
   257	            continue
   258	        by_chain.setdefault(tuple(chain.introns), set()).add(rid)
   259	
   260	    # Read spans (0-based [start, end)) -- needed by the span-guarded fold and the
   261	    # mono-exon fold. Built once, only when a span-aware step is enabled.
   262	    read_span: Dict[str, Tuple[int, int]] = {}
   263	    if fold_span_guard or fold_monoexon_contained:
   264	        for rd, _chain in read_chains:
   265	            rid = rd.get("query_name")
   266	            rs = rd.get("reference_start")
   267	            re_ = rd.get("reference_end")
   268	            if rid is not None and rs is not None and re_ is not None:
   269	                read_span[rid] = (rs, re_)
   270	
   271	    # 2. collapse every exact contiguous sub-chain into its longest exact container.
   272	    #    Default: UNCONDITIONAL (no read-support guard). With fold_span_guard, a read
   273	    #    folds ONLY if its aligned span does not run exonically across one of the
   274	    #    container's EXTRA introns (retained-intron / alternative isoform reads are kept
   275	    #    as their own candidate instead of being absorbed). Whether a *consistent*
   276	    #    folded sub-chain is a genuine short isoform is still a SEPARATE downstream
   277	    #    recovery step (read-end pileup), never guessed here from read counts.
   278	    chains_sorted = sorted(by_chain, key=lambda c: (-len(c), -len(by_chain[c]), c))
   279	    kept: List[Chain] = []
   280	    reads: Dict[Chain, Set[str]] = {}
   281	    folded_of: Dict[Chain, List[Tuple[Chain, Set[str]]]] = {}  # container -> shadows
   282	    for c in chains_sorted:
   283	        container = next((k for k in kept if _exact_subchain(c, k)), None)
   284	        if container is None:
   285	            kept.append(c)
   286	            reads[c] = set(by_chain[c])
   287	            continue
   288	        if not fold_span_guard:
   289	            reads[container] |= by_chain[c]
   290	            folded_of.setdefault(container, []).append((c, set(by_chain[c])))
   291	            continue
   292	        # span guard: split c's reads into those consistent with the container (fold)
   293	        # and those exonic across one of the container's extra introns (keep as c).
   294	        off = _subchain_offset(c, container)
   295	        extra = container[:off] + container[off + len(c):]
   296	        fold_r: Set[str] = set()
   297	        keep_r: Set[str] = set()
   298	        for rid in by_chain[c]:
   299	            sp = read_span.get(rid)
   300	            if sp is not None and any(sp[0] <= d and sp[1] >= a for d, a in extra):
   301	                keep_r.add(rid)            # exonic across an extra intron -> distinct
   302	            else:
   303	                fold_r.add(rid)
   304	        if fold_r:
   305	            reads[container] |= fold_r
   306	            folded_of.setdefault(container, []).append((c, fold_r))
   307	        if keep_r:
   308	            kept.append(c)
   309	            reads[c] = keep_r
   310	
   311	    # 3. union-find the survivors by wobble / cassette / containment (multi-exon only).
   312	    multi = [c for c in kept if len(c) >= 1]
   313	    mono = [c for c in kept if len(c) == 0]
   314	
   315	    # 3b. (optional) fold single-exon reads that are wholly inside a multi-exon
   316	    #     candidate's exon into that candidate -- 5'/3' degradation fragments of a
   317	    #     spliced transcript, otherwise emitted as standalone mono FPs. Per-read (the
   318	    #     mono chain pools every chain-less read), deterministic container choice.
   319	    if fold_monoexon_contained and mono and multi:
   320	        multi_span: Dict[Chain, Tuple[int, int]] = {}
   321	        for c in multi:
   322	            pts = [read_span[r] for r in reads[c] if r in read_span]
   323	            if pts:
   324	                multi_span[c] = (min(p[0] for p in pts), max(p[1] for p in pts))
   325	        containers = [c for c in multi if c in multi_span]
   326	        for mc in mono:
   327	            remaining: Set[str] = set()
   328	            for rid in reads[mc]:
   329	                sp = read_span.get(rid)
   330	                if sp is None:
   331	                    remaining.add(rid)
   332	                    continue
   333	                s, e = sp
   334	                hits = [c for c in containers
   335	                        if _monoexon_in_exon(s, e, c, *multi_span[c])]
   336	                if not hits:
   337	                    remaining.add(rid)
   338	                    continue
   339	                best = max(hits, key=lambda c: (len(reads[c]), len(c), c))
   340	                reads[best].add(rid)
   341	                folded_of.setdefault(best, []).append(((), {rid}))
   342	            reads[mc] = remaining
   343	        mono = [c for c in mono if reads[c]]
   344	
   345	    parent = {c: c for c in multi}
   346	
   347	    def find(x: Chain) -> Chain:
   348	        while parent[x] != x:
   349	            parent[x] = parent[parent[x]]
   350	            x = parent[x]
   351	        return x
   352	
   353	    for i in range(len(multi)):
   354	        for j in range(i + 1, len(multi)):
   355	            a, b = multi[i], multi[j]
   356	            if find(a) != find(b) and _related(a, b, wobble_bp, cassette_max_exon_bp):
   357	                parent[find(a)] = find(b)
   358	
   359	    comps: Dict[Chain, List[Chain]] = {}
   360	    for c in multi:
   361	        comps.setdefault(find(c), []).append(c)
   362	
   363	    def _mk(c: Chain) -> GenCandidate:
   364	        """Member candidate with its folded exact-sub-chain shadows attached."""
   365	        return GenCandidate(
   366	            IntronChain(introns=c), set(reads[c]),
   367	            folded=[GenCandidate(IntronChain(introns=fc), set(fr))
   368	                    for fc, fr in folded_of.get(c, [])])
   369	
   370	    clusters: List[ChainCluster] = []
   371	    for comp in comps.values():
   372	        members = [_mk(c) for c in comp]
   373	        union: Set[str] = set()
   374	        for c in comp:
   375	            union |= reads[c]
   376	        clusters.append(ChainCluster(members=members, read_ids=union))
   377	
   378	    for c in mono:  # mono singletons
   379	        clusters.append(ChainCluster(members=[_mk(c)], read_ids=set(reads[c])))
   380	
   381	    return clusters
   382	
   383	
   384	def _family_sort_key(fam: ChainFamily):
   385	    """Total, deterministic ordering key over a family's chains (read variants + GTF members).
   386	
   387	    Every family has >=1 chain, so ``min`` is well-defined even for a GTF-only family (empty
   388	    ``variants``). The extra components make the order total under any (near-impossible) min tie.
   389	    """
   390	    chains = list(fam.variants) + [c for _, c in fam.gtf_members]
   391	    return (min(chains), tuple(fam.variants), tuple(sorted(g for g, _ in fam.gtf_members)))
   392	
   393	
   394	def _attach_gtf_variants(
   395	    families: List[ChainFamily],
   396	    gtf_variants: List[Tuple[str, Chain]],
   397	    wobble_bp: int,
   398	    cassette_max_exon_bp: int,
   399	) -> None:
   400	    """Attach each GTF hypothesis to the single most-related read family (in place).
   401	
   402	    NON-BRIDGING: a GTF is added to at most ONE family and never merges two read families. A GTF
   403	    related to no read family becomes its own GTF-only family (empty read pool). Mono (empty-chain)
   404	    GTF entries are skipped (handled by the later mono finalizer). Deterministic: GTF entries are
   405	    processed in sorted order and the best family is chosen by (most related variants, then smallest
   406	    first-variant coords). GTF is a zero-read hypothesis: it is NEVER added to any ``read_pool`` or
   407	    ``variant_reads``.
   408	    """
   409	    read_families = [f for f in families if f.variants]   # snapshot: a GTF-only family never attracts GTF
   410	    gtf_only: List[ChainFamily] = []
   411	    for gid, gchain in sorted(gtf_variants, key=lambda gv: (gv[1], gv[0])):
   412	        if not gchain:                     # mono / empty-chain GTF -> deferred to the mono finalizer
   413	            continue
   414	        best = None                        # (n_related, first_variant, family)
   415	        for fam in read_families:
   416	            n = sum(1 for v in fam.variants
   417	                    if _related(gchain, v, wobble_bp, cassette_max_exon_bp))
   418	            if n == 0:
   419	                continue
   420	            if best is None or n > best[0] or (n == best[0] and fam.variants[0] < best[1]):
   421	                best = (n, fam.variants[0], fam)
   422	        if best is not None:
   423	            best[2].gtf_members.append((gid, gchain))
   424	        else:
   425	            gtf_only.append(ChainFamily(
   426	                variants=[], read_pool=set(), variant_reads={},
   427	                gtf_members=[(gid, gchain)]))
   428	    families.extend(gtf_only)
   429	
   430	
   431	def cluster_families(
   432	    read_chains: List[Tuple[Dict, IntronChain]],
   433	    *,
   434	    wobble_bp: int = 6,
   435	    cassette_max_exon_bp: int = 70,
   436	    gtf_variants: Optional[List[Tuple[str, Chain]]] = None,
   437	) -> ClusterResult:
   438	    """Clustering ONLY: group an interval's reads into multi-exon structural families.
   439	
   440	    The clean, single-responsibility clustering step (see the clustering redesign). Unlike
   441	    :func:`cluster_read_chains`, it does NOT fold exact sub-chains, does NOT fold or spatially
   442	    split mono reads, and never rewrites coordinates. It ONLY:
   443	
   444	      1. groups reads by their EXACT intron chain (3' ignored, no snap),
   445	      2. buckets every single-exon (chain-less) read into ``mono_reads`` UNTOUCHED,
   446	      3. union-finds the distinct multi-exon chains into families by wobble / cassette /
   447	         containment (single-linkage; see :func:`_related`),
   448	      4. returns each family's variant chains + a POOLED read set (reads belong to the
   449	         family, not a member) + the per-variant discovery provenance,
   450	      5. (optional) attaches each GTF hypothesis to the single best-related read family as a
   451	         ZERO-read ``gtf_member`` -- NON-BRIDGING (a GTF never merges two read families) and
   452	         never a read-pool source; a GTF matching no read family becomes a GTF-only family.
   453	
   454	    Deterministic: families and their variants are coordinate-sorted; ``mono_reads`` is a
   455	    plain set. Fold, path-completion, and mono resolution are SEPARATE later per-cluster
   456	    steps -- this function makes no structural-collapse or read-ownership decision.
   457	
   458	    Args:
   459	        read_chains: per-read ``(read_dict, IntronChain)`` (read_dict needs "query_name";
   460	            CIGAR-derived chains, used as-is).
   461	        wobble_bp: per-junction tolerance for wobble / cassette / containment joins.
   462	        cassette_max_exon_bp: max skipped-exon length for a cassette join (0 disables).
   463	        gtf_variants: optional annotation hypotheses as ``(gtf_id, intron_chain)`` pairs. Each is
   464	            attached to the single most-related read family (by the same ``_related`` predicate) as a
   465	            zero-read ``gtf_member``; it NEVER bridges two read families and NEVER contributes to a
   466	            ``read_pool``. Mono (empty-chain) GTF entries are ignored here (annotation of single-exon
   467	            transcripts is handled by the later mono finalizer). The read families are formed BEFORE
   468	            attachment, so annotation cannot change read-derived family structure.
   469	
   470	    Returns:
   471	        A :class:`ClusterResult` (multi-exon families + the interval mono bucket).
   472	    """
   473	    # 1. group reads by EXACT chain; single-exon (chain-less) reads -> the mono bucket.
   474	    by_chain: Dict[Chain, Set[str]] = {}
   475	    mono_reads: Set[str] = set()
   476	    for rd, chain in read_chains:
   477	        rid = rd.get("query_name")
   478	        if rid is None:
   479	            continue
   480	        introns = tuple(chain.introns)
   481	        if not introns:
   482	            mono_reads.add(rid)
   483	        else:
   484	            by_chain.setdefault(introns, set()).add(rid)
   485	
   486	    # 2. union-find the distinct multi-exon chains by wobble / cassette / containment.
   487	    #    Sorted vertex order -> deterministic; the resulting partition (connected
   488	    #    components of the symmetric _related graph) is order-independent regardless.
   489	    multi = sorted(by_chain)
   490	    parent: Dict[Chain, Chain] = {c: c for c in multi}
   491	
   492	    def find(x: Chain) -> Chain:
   493	        while parent[x] != x:
   494	            parent[x] = parent[parent[x]]
   495	            x = parent[x]
   496	        return x
   497	
   498	    for i in range(len(multi)):
   499	        for j in range(i + 1, len(multi)):
   500	            a, b = multi[i], multi[j]
   501	            if find(a) != find(b) and _related(a, b, wobble_bp, cassette_max_exon_bp):
   502	                parent[find(a)] = find(b)
   503	
   504	    comps: Dict[Chain, List[Chain]] = {}
   505	    for c in multi:
   506	        comps.setdefault(find(c), []).append(c)
   507	
   508	    # 3. build families: variants coord-sorted, read pool = union of members' reads,
   509	    #    per-variant discovery reads kept as provenance (not final ownership).
   510	    families: List[ChainFamily] = []
   511	    for comp in comps.values():
   512	        variants = sorted(comp)
   513	        pool: Set[str] = set()
   514	        for c in comp:
   515	            pool |= by_chain[c]
   516	        families.append(ChainFamily(
   517	            variants=variants,
   518	            read_pool=pool,
   519	            variant_reads={c: set(by_chain[c]) for c in comp},
   520	        ))
   521	
   522	    # 4. (optional) attach GTF hypotheses to read families -- NON-BRIDGING, zero-read.
   523	    if gtf_variants:
   524	        _attach_gtf_variants(families, gtf_variants, wobble_bp, cassette_max_exon_bp)
   525	
   526	    # Stable, total family order over every family's chains (read variants + gtf members),
   527	    # so GTF-only families (empty ``variants``) also sort deterministically.
   528	    families.sort(key=_family_sort_key)
   529	
   530	    return ClusterResult(families=families, mono_reads=mono_reads)

    25	def _generate_novel_id() -> str:
    26	    """Generate a short unique ID for a novel candidate."""
    27	    import uuid
    28	
    29	    return "novel_" + uuid.uuid4().hex[:8]

    32	def _intron_chains_match(a: IntronChain, b: IntronChain) -> bool:
    33	    """Check if two intron chains are identical."""
    34	    return a.introns == b.introns

    36	
    37	def _spatial_read_clusters(read_ids, spans):
    38	    """Group chain-less (single-exon) reads into non-overlapping genomic loci via
    39	    single-linkage over span overlap. Without this, every mono read in an interval
    40	    pools into ONE candidate whose union span bridges distinct single-exon genes.
    41	    Reads absent from ``spans`` are dropped. Returns a list of read-id lists."""
    42	    items = sorted((spans[r][0], spans[r][1], r) for r in read_ids if r in spans)
    43	    clusters = []
    44	    cur = []
    45	    cur_end = None
    46	    for s, e, r in items:
    47	        if cur and s > cur_end:                 # no overlap with the current locus
    48	            clusters.append([x[2] for x in cur])
    49	            cur = []
    50	            cur_end = None
    51	        cur.append((s, e, r))
    52	        cur_end = e if cur_end is None else max(cur_end, e)
    53	    if cur:
    54	        clusters.append([x[2] for x in cur])
    55	    return clusters
    56	
    57	
    58	def _best_overlap_gtf(mono_gtf, lo: int, hi: int):
    59	    """Pick the single-exon GTF candidate whose span most overlaps ``[lo, hi)``.
    60	
    61	    Mono (empty-chain) candidates must NOT be matched to GTF by chain equality -- every
    62	    single-exon transcript shares the empty chain, so a dict keyed by chain collapses
    63	    them and routes all single-exon reads to whichever mono GTF was inserted first.
    64	    Match by genomic overlap instead; returns None when nothing overlaps."""
    65	    best = None
    66	    best_ov = 0
    67	    for gc, gs, ge in mono_gtf:
    68	        ov = min(hi, ge) - max(lo, gs)
    69	        if ov > best_ov:
    70	            best_ov = ov
    71	            best = gc
    72	    return best
    73	
    74	
    75	_COMPLEMENT = str.maketrans("ACGTNacgtn", "TGCANtgcan")
    76	
    77	
    78	def _reverse_complement(seq: str) -> str:
    79	    return seq.translate(_COMPLEMENT)[::-1]
    80	
    81	
    82	def _exons_from_chain(start: int, end: int, chain: IntronChain) -> List[tuple]:
    83	    """Derive exon (start, end) blocks from an intron chain spanning [start, end)."""
    84	    introns = chain.introns
    85	    if not introns:
    86	        return [(start, end)]
    87	    exons = [(start, introns[0][0])]
    88	    for i in range(len(introns) - 1):
    89	        exons.append((introns[i][1], introns[i + 1][0]))
    90	    exons.append((introns[-1][1], end))
    91	    # Drop any zero-length blocks defensively
    92	    return [(s, e) for s, e in exons if e > s]
    93	
    94	
    95	def _build_spliced_sequence(
    96	    genome_fasta: str,
    97	    start: int,
    98	    end: int,
    99	    chain: IntronChain,
   100	    strand: str,
   101	) -> str:
   102	    """Stitch a spliced transcript sequence from a genome chromosome string.
   103	
   104	    Args:
   105	        genome_fasta: Full chromosome sequence string.
   106	        start: 0-based transcript start (left edge of first exon).
   107	        end: 0-based exclusive transcript end (right edge of last exon).
   108	        chain: Intron chain in genomic coordinates.
   109	        strand: '+' or '-'. Reverse complement is applied for '-'.
   110	
   111	    Returns:
   112	        Spliced cDNA sequence, or empty string when bounds are unusable.
   113	    """
   114	    if not genome_fasta or end <= start:
   115	        return ""
   116	    chrom_len = len(genome_fasta)
   117	    # Clip to chromosome bounds.
   118	    s = max(0, min(start, chrom_len))
   119	    e = max(0, min(end, chrom_len))
   120	    if e <= s:
   121	        return ""
   122	    parts = []
   123	    for ex_s, ex_e in _exons_from_chain(s, e, chain):
   124	        ex_s_c = max(s, min(ex_s, chrom_len))
   125	        ex_e_c = max(s, min(ex_e, chrom_len))
   126	        if ex_e_c > ex_s_c:
   127	            parts.append(genome_fasta[ex_s_c:ex_e_c])
   128	    seq = "".join(parts)
   129	    if strand == "-":
   130	        seq = _reverse_complement(seq)
   131	    return seq
   132	
   133	
   134	def _three_prime_within_threshold(pos_a: int, pos_b: int, threshold: int) -> bool:
   135	    return abs(pos_a - pos_b) <= threshold
   136	
   137	
   138	def _graph_groups(non_fusion_reads, strand, tol, min_edge_reads,
   139	                  tss_brake=True, tss_tol=20, tss_min_reads=3, tss_frac=0.4):
   140	    """Build novel-candidate read groups via intron-graph assembly: extend each
   141	    read's chain to a maximal read-supported chain (:func:`assemble_chains`),
   142	    group reads by that extended chain, and key each group by (TES, IntronChain)
   143	    for the shared candidate-creation path. TES = the group's 3' terminus (max
   144	    reference_end on '+', min reference_start on '-'). Truncated reads on the same
   145	    maximal path land in ONE group -> one full-length candidate.
   146	
   147	    ``tss_brake`` passes each read's 5'-end position to the assembler so genuine
   148	    short isoforms sitting at a real TSS peak are not extended (merged) away."""
   149	    from fin.candidates.junction_graph import assemble_chains
   150	
   151	    reads_kept = []
   152	    chains = []
   153	    five_prime_pos = []
   154	    for rd in non_fusion_reads:
   155	        cigar = rd.get("cigartuples")
   156	        ref_start = rd.get("reference_start")
   157	        if cigar is None or ref_start is None:
   158	            continue
   159	        reads_kept.append(rd)
   160	        chains.append(tuple(extract_intron_chain(cigar, ref_start).introns))
   161	        # read 5'-end = the truncation-prone terminus (strand-aware)
   162	        five_prime_pos.append(rd.get("reference_end") if strand == "-" else ref_start)
   163	    if not chains:
   164	        return {}
   165	    _, extended = assemble_chains(
   166	        chains, tol, min_edge_reads,
   167	        five_prime_pos=(five_prime_pos if tss_brake else None),
   168	        strand=strand, tss_tol=tss_tol,
   169	        min_tss_reads=tss_min_reads, tss_frac=tss_frac,
   170	    )
   171	
   172	    by_chain: dict = {}
   173	    for i, rd in enumerate(reads_kept):
   174	        by_chain.setdefault(extended[i], []).append(rd)
   175	
   176	    groups: dict = {}
   177	    for ext_chain, reads in by_chain.items():
   178	        if strand == "-":
   179	            tes = min((rd.get("reference_start", 0) for rd in reads), default=0)
   180	        else:
   181	            tes = max((rd.get("reference_end", 0) for rd in reads), default=0)
   182	        groups[(tes, IntronChain(introns=ext_chain))] = reads
   183	    return groups
   184	
   185	
   186	def _chain_cluster_candidates(
   187	    interval, non_fusion_reads, all_read_ids, read_sequences, gtf_reader,
   188	    genome_fasta, strand, min_novel_reads, *, wobble_bp, cassette_max_exon_bp,
   189	    fold_monoexon_contained=False, fold_span_guard=False,
   190	) -> CandidateSet:
   191	    """NEW generation: build candidates via intron-chain clustering.
   192	
   193	    Group reads by intron chain (3' ignored), fold EXACT sub-chains, cluster
   194	    wobble/cassette/containment (members kept); each surviving member chain is a NOVEL
   195	    candidate whose span is the UNION of its reads' extents and whose sequence is
   196	    stitched from the genome. A novel chain that EXACTLY matches a GTF transcript's
   197	    chain merges its reads into that GTF candidate instead. No canonical search.
   198	    """
   199	    from fin.candidates.chain_cluster import cluster_read_chains
   200	
   201	    read_chains = []
   202	    spans: dict = {}
   203	    for rd in non_fusion_reads:
   204	        cigar = rd.get("cigartuples")
   205	        ref_start = rd.get("reference_start")
   206	        if cigar is None or ref_start is None:
   207	            continue
   208	        read_chains.append((rd, extract_intron_chain(cigar, ref_start)))
   209	        q = rd.get("query_name")
   210	        if q is not None:
   211	            spans[q] = (ref_start, rd.get("reference_end", ref_start))
   212	
   213	    clusters = cluster_read_chains(
   214	        read_chains, wobble_bp=wobble_bp, cassette_max_exon_bp=cassette_max_exon_bp,
   215	        fold_monoexon_contained=fold_monoexon_contained,
   216	        fold_span_guard=fold_span_guard)
   217	
   218	    # GTF candidates (identical to the legacy path).
   219	    gtf_candidates: List[TranscriptCandidate] = []
   220	    gtf_by_chain: dict = {}
   221	    if gtf_reader is not None:
   222	        for tx in gtf_reader.get_transcripts_in_region(
   223	                interval.chrom, interval.start, interval.end):
   224	            # Interval is strand-separated (reads are strand-filtered into it), so an
   225	            # opposite-strand annotation must NOT enter and compete for reads / absorb
   226	            # same-chain support. (No effect when the interval is unstranded or there is
   227	            # no GTF, e.g. p00.)
   228	            if interval.strand is not None and tx.strand != interval.strand:
   229	                continue
   230	            chain = gtf_transcript_to_intron_chain(tx)
   231	            tx.sort_features()
   232	            three_prime = tx.end if tx.strand == "+" else tx.start
   233	            gc = TranscriptCandidate(
   234	                candidate_id=tx.transcript_id, intron_chain=chain,
   235	                three_prime_pos=three_prime, sequence=tx.get_spliced_sequence(genome_fasta),
   236	                source="gtf", supporting_read_ids=set(), chrom=interval.chrom,
   237	                strand=tx.strand, start=tx.start, end=tx.end)
   238	            gtf_candidates.append(gc)
   239	            gtf_by_chain.setdefault(chain, gc)   # first GTF wins on an exact-chain tie
   240	
   241	    # Single-exon (empty-chain) GTF transcripts, matched to mono members by genomic
   242	    # overlap (NOT by empty-chain equality, which would collapse them all to one).
   243	    mono_gtf = [(gc, gc.start, gc.end)
   244	                for gc in gtf_candidates if not gc.intron_chain.introns]
   245	
   246	    # Record each ChainCluster's members by candidate_id (NOT list index): downstream
   247	    # gates (canonical_gate, junction-dominance, fusion) drop/reorder candidate_set
   248	    # candidates after discovery, so an id survives those mutations where an index
   249	    # would go stale. A member with no candidate (empty span/seq) contributes nothing.
   250	    candidates: List[TranscriptCandidate] = list(gtf_candidates)
   251	    cluster_id_lists: List[List[str]] = []
   252	    # container candidate_id -> its folded SHADOW sub-chains (for 5'-TSS recovery).
   253	    # Each shadow: (intron_chain_tuple, sorted_read_id_tuple). GTF ids can recur
   254	    # across clusters (same annotation matched twice) -> merge the shadow lists.
   255	    shadow_map: Dict[str, List[Tuple[Tuple[Tuple[int, int], ...], Tuple[str, ...]]]] = {}
   256	    def _emit(chain, member_reads, start, end, gc):
   257	        """Merge ``member_reads`` into GTF ``gc`` (if any) else emit a novel candidate.
   258	        Returns the candidate_id, or None when the spliced sequence cannot be built."""
   259	        if gc is not None:                        # GTF match -> merge
   260	            gc.supporting_read_ids.update(member_reads)
   261	            return gc.candidate_id
   262	        seq = _build_spliced_sequence(genome_fasta, start, end, chain, strand)
   263	        if not seq:
   264	            return None
   265	        cid = _generate_novel_id()
   266	        candidates.append(TranscriptCandidate(
   267	            candidate_id=cid, intron_chain=chain,
   268	            three_prime_pos=(end if strand == "+" else start), sequence=seq,
   269	            source="novel", supporting_read_ids=set(member_reads),
   270	            chrom=interval.chrom, strand=strand, start=start, end=end))
   271	        return cid
   272	
   273	    for cl in clusters:
   274	        ids: List[str] = []
   275	        for m in cl.members:
   276	            if m.chain.introns:                   # multi-exon: exact-chain GTF match
   277	                starts = [spans[r][0] for r in m.read_ids if r in spans]
   278	                ends = [spans[r][1] for r in m.read_ids if r in spans]
   279	                if not starts:
   280	                    continue
   281	                cand_id = _emit(m.chain, m.read_ids, min(starts), max(ends),
   282	                                gtf_by_chain.get(m.chain))
   283	                if cand_id is None:
   284	                    continue
   285	                ids.append(cand_id)
   286	                # folded exact-sub-chain shadows keyed by the candidate it became
   287	                # (5'-TSS recovery). Mono members carry none.
   288	                member_shadows = [
   289	                    (tuple(f.chain.introns), tuple(sorted(f.read_ids)))
   290	                    for f in m.folded if f.chain.introns
   291	                ]
   292	                if member_shadows:
   293	                    shadow_map.setdefault(cand_id, []).extend(member_shadows)
   294	            else:
   295	                # mono (single-exon): split the pooled chain-less reads into per-locus
   296	                # groups so distinct single-exon genes are not bridged, then overlap-match
   297	                # each locus to a single-exon GTF (else emit a per-locus novel candidate).
   298	                for group in _spatial_read_clusters(m.read_ids, spans):
   299	                    gs = [spans[r][0] for r in group]
   300	                    ge = [spans[r][1] for r in group]
   301	                    lo, hi = min(gs), max(ge)
   302	                    cand_id = _emit(m.chain, set(group), lo, hi,
   303	                                    _best_overlap_gtf(mono_gtf, lo, hi))
   304	                    if cand_id is not None:
   305	                        ids.append(cand_id)
   306	        cluster_id_lists.append(ids)
   307	
   308	    # Drop low-support novels; prune cluster lists to surviving candidate ids.
   309	    if min_novel_reads > 1:
   310	        candidates = [c for c in candidates
   311	                      if c.source != "novel"
   312	                      or len(c.supporting_read_ids) >= min_novel_reads]
   313	        keep_ids = {c.candidate_id for c in candidates}
   314	        cluster_id_lists = [
   315	            [cid for cid in ids if cid in keep_ids] for ids in cluster_id_lists
   316	        ]
   317	        # Prune shadow entries whose container candidate was dropped.
   318	        shadow_map = {cid: sh for cid, sh in shadow_map.items() if cid in keep_ids}
   319	
   320	    logger.info(
   321	        "Interval %s: %d candidates (%d GTF, %d novel) from %d reads [chain-cluster]",
   322	        interval.region_string, len(candidates),
   323	        sum(1 for c in candidates if c.source == "gtf"),
   324	        sum(1 for c in candidates if c.source == "novel"), len(all_read_ids))
   325	
   326	    return CandidateSet(
   327	        interval=interval, candidates=candidates, read_ids=all_read_ids,
   328	        read_sequences=read_sequences, clusters=cluster_id_lists,
   329	        shadows=shadow_map, read_spans=spans)
   330	
   331	
   332	def discover_candidates(
   333	    interval: GenomicInterval,
   334	    bam_path: str,
   335	    gtf_reader,
   336	    genome_fasta: str,
   337	    threshold: int = 24,
   338	    min_novel_reads: int = 1,
   339	    canonical_search_bp: int = 0,
   340	    max_chains_per_read: int = 16,
   341	    canonical_motifs: tuple = ("GT-AG", "GC-AG", "AT-AC"),
   342	    denovo_wobble_tol: int = 0,
   343	    denovo_wobble_shadow_ratio: float = 1.0,
   344	    denovo_graph: bool = False,
   345	    denovo_graph_tol: int = 6,
   346	    denovo_graph_min_edge_reads: int = 2,
   347	    denovo_graph_tss_brake: bool = True,
   348	    denovo_graph_tss_tol: int = 20,
   349	    denovo_graph_tss_min_reads: int = 3,
   350	    denovo_graph_tss_frac: float = 0.4,
   351	    chain_cluster: bool = False,
   352	    chain_cluster_wobble_bp: int = 6,
   353	    chain_cluster_cassette_max_exon_bp: int = 70,
   354	    chain_cluster_fold_monoexon: bool = False,
   355	    chain_cluster_fold_span_guard: bool = False,
   356	) -> CandidateSet:
   357	    """Discover transcript candidates for a genomic interval.
   358	
   359	    Combines GTF-annotated transcripts with novel isoforms discovered
   360	    from read alignments using intron chain + 3' end clustering.
   361	
   362	    Args:
   363	        interval: Genomic interval to process.
   364	        bam_path: Path to BAM file.
   365	        gtf_reader: Opened GTFReader instance (already parsed).
   366	        genome_fasta: Full chromosome sequence string for the interval's chromosome.
   367	        threshold: 3' end clustering distance in bp.
   368	        canonical_search_bp: If > 0, for every read scan ±N bp around each
   369	            junction's CIGAR position for canonical GT-AG (donor/acceptor)
   370	            motifs and emit the resulting chain alternatives in addition to
   371	            the original chain. 0 disables.
   372	        max_chains_per_read: Hard cap on the per-read alternative chain count
   373	            during canonical search. Reads whose Cartesian-product expansion
   374	            exceeds the cap fall back to their original chain.
   375	
   376	    Returns:
   377	        CandidateSet with discovered candidates.
   378	    """
   379	    from fin.io.io_bam import BamReader
   380	
   381	    # 1. Fetch reads for interval, skip fusion reads
   382	    all_read_ids: Set[str] = set()
   383	    non_fusion_reads = []
   384	    read_sequences: dict = {}
   385	
   386	    with BamReader(bam_path) as bam:
   387	        for alignment in bam.fetch(
   388	            reference=interval.chrom, start=interval.start, end=interval.end
   389	        ):
   390	            rd = bam.alignment_to_dict(alignment)
   391	            if not rd or not rd.get("is_mapped"):
   392	                continue
   393	            # Honor strand-separated intervals. bam.fetch returns reads on BOTH
   394	            # strands overlapping the region, but each read belongs to its own
   395	            # mapped strand's interval. Without this guard an antisense interval
   396	            # that spatially contains a sense interval swallows the sense reads,
   397	            # corrupting strand assignment (a candidate emitted on the wrong
   398	            # strand can never match) and read-to-candidate quantification.
   399	            if interval.strand is not None:
