--- cluster_families signature/docs ---
   431	def cluster_families(
   432	    read_chains: List[Tuple[Dict, IntronChain]],
   433	    *,
   434	    wobble_bp: int = 6,
   435	    cassette_max_exon_bp: int = 70,
   436	    gtf_variants: Optional[List[Tuple[str, Chain]]] = None,
   437	) -> ClusterResult:
   438	    """Clustering ONLY: group an interval's reads into multi-exon structural families.
   439	
   440	    The clean, single-responsibility clustering step (see the clustering redesign). Unlike
   441	    :func:`cluster_read_chains`, it does NOT fold exact sub-chains, does NOT fold or spatially
   442	    split mono reads, and never rewrites coordinates. It ONLY:
   443	
   444	      1. groups reads by their EXACT intron chain (3' ignored, no snap),
   445	      2. buckets every single-exon (chain-less) read into ``mono_reads`` UNTOUCHED,
   446	      3. union-finds the distinct multi-exon chains into families by wobble / cassette /
   447	         containment (single-linkage; see :func:`_related`),
   448	      4. returns each family's variant chains + a POOLED read set (reads belong to the
   449	         family, not a member) + the per-variant discovery provenance,
   450	      5. (optional) attaches each GTF hypothesis to the single best-related read family as a
   451	         ZERO-read ``gtf_member`` -- NON-BRIDGING (a GTF never merges two read families) and
   452	         never a read-pool source; a GTF matching no read family becomes a GTF-only family.
   453	
   454	    Deterministic: families and their variants are coordinate-sorted; ``mono_reads`` is a
   455	    plain set. Fold, path-completion, and mono resolution are SEPARATE later per-cluster
   456	    steps -- this function makes no structural-collapse or read-ownership decision.
   457	
   458	    Args:
   459	        read_chains: per-read ``(read_dict, IntronChain)`` (read_dict needs "query_name";
   460	            CIGAR-derived chains, used as-is).
   461	        wobble_bp: per-junction tolerance for wobble / cassette / containment joins.
   462	        cassette_max_exon_bp: max skipped-exon length for a cassette join (0 disables).
   463	        gtf_variants: optional annotation hypotheses as ``(gtf_id, intron_chain)`` pairs. Each is
   464	            attached to the single most-related read family (by the same ``_related`` predicate) as a
   465	            zero-read ``gtf_member``; it NEVER bridges two read families and NEVER contributes to a
   466	            ``read_pool``. Mono (empty-chain) GTF entries are ignored here (annotation of single-exon
   467	            transcripts is handled by the later mono finalizer). The read families are formed BEFORE
   468	            attachment, so annotation cannot change read-derived family structure.
   469	
   470	    Returns:
   471	        A :class:`ClusterResult` (multi-exon families + the interval mono bucket).
   472	    """
   473	    # 1. group reads by EXACT chain; single-exon (chain-less) reads -> the mono bucket.
   474	    by_chain: Dict[Chain, Set[str]] = {}
   475	    mono_reads: Set[str] = set()
   476	    for rd, chain in read_chains:
   477	        rid = rd.get("query_name")
   478	        if rid is None:
   479	            continue
   480	        introns = tuple(chain.introns)
   481	        if not introns:
   482	            mono_reads.add(rid)
   483	        else:
   484	            by_chain.setdefault(introns, set()).add(rid)
   485	
   486	    # 2. union-find the distinct multi-exon chains by wobble / cassette / containment.
   487	    #    Sorted vertex order -> deterministic; the resulting partition (connected
   488	    #    components of the symmetric _related graph) is order-independent regardless.
   489	    multi = sorted(by_chain)
   490	    parent: Dict[Chain, Chain] = {c: c for c in multi}
   491	
   492	    def find(x: Chain) -> Chain:
   493	        while parent[x] != x:
   494	            parent[x] = parent[parent[x]]
   495	            x = parent[x]
   496	        return x
   497	
   498	    for i in range(len(multi)):
   499	        for j in range(i + 1, len(multi)):
   500	            a, b = multi[i], multi[j]
   501	            if find(a) != find(b) and _related(a, b, wobble_bp, cassette_max_exon_bp):
   502	                parent[find(a)] = find(b)
   503	
   504	    comps: Dict[Chain, List[Chain]] = {}
   505	    for c in multi:
   506	        comps.setdefault(find(c), []).append(c)
   507	
   508	    # 3. build families: variants coord-sorted, read pool = union of members' reads,
   509	    #    per-variant discovery reads kept as provenance (not final ownership).
   510	    families: List[ChainFamily] = []
   511	    for comp in comps.values():
   512	        variants = sorted(comp)
   513	        pool: Set[str] = set()
   514	        for c in comp:
   515	            pool |= by_chain[c]
   516	        families.append(ChainFamily(
   517	            variants=variants,
   518	            read_pool=pool,
   519	            variant_reads={c: set(by_chain[c]) for c in comp},
   520	        ))
   521	
   522	    # 4. (optional) attach GTF hypotheses to read families -- NON-BRIDGING, zero-read.
   523	    if gtf_variants:
   524	        _attach_gtf_variants(families, gtf_variants, wobble_bp, cassette_max_exon_bp)
   525	
   526	    # Stable, total family order over every family's chains (read variants + gtf members),
   527	    # so GTF-only families (empty ``variants``) also sort deterministically.
   528	    families.sort(key=_family_sort_key)
   529	
   530	    return ClusterResult(families=families, mono_reads=mono_reads)
--- graph full ---
     1	"""De-novo intron graph: assemble full-length transcript intron chains from
     2	partial/truncated read chains.
     3	
     4	Measured motivation (p00, no annotation): pyfin's dominant de-novo error is
     5	truncation — candidates that are a contiguous sub-chain of a real transcript
     6	(gffcompare class 'c', ~16% of output), because dRNA reads are 3'-biased so a
     7	read covers only the 3' junctions. pyfin currently emits each read's exact chain
     8	as a candidate, so a truncated read -> a truncated candidate.
     9	
    10	This module pools all reads' junctions, clusters wobbled junctions to a
    11	read-count consensus, builds a directed read-adjacency graph (nodes = consensus
    12	junctions ordered by genomic coord; edges = "these two junctions are consecutive
    13	in >= min_edge_reads reads"), and EXTENDS each read's chain through
    14	UNAMBIGUOUSLY-supported edges to a maximal chain. Extension stops at genuine
    15	branch points (a node with >1 supported successor), so it lengthens truncated
    16	chains WITHOUT fabricating wrong junction combinations.
    17	
    18	NOT annotation-snap: consensus coordinates and edges come only from reads.
    19	"""
    20	from __future__ import annotations
    21	
    22	from collections import Counter, defaultdict
    23	from typing import Dict, List, Set, Tuple
    24	
    25	Junction = Tuple[int, int]
    26	Chain = Tuple[Junction, ...]
    27	
    28	
    29	def cluster_junctions(chains: List[Chain], tol: int) -> Dict[Junction, Junction]:
    30	    """Map every observed junction to a consensus junction. Greedy by read count:
    31	    the most-observed junction seeds a centroid; a junction within ``tol`` bp on
    32	    BOTH donor and acceptor maps to the first matching centroid. Deterministic
    33	    (count desc, then coordinate)."""
    34	    counts: Counter = Counter()
    35	    for ch in chains:
    36	        for j in ch:
    37	            counts[j] += 1
    38	    consensus: Dict[Junction, Junction] = {}
    39	    centroids: List[Junction] = []
    40	    for j in sorted(counts, key=lambda x: (-counts[x], x)):
    41	        hit = None
    42	        for cj in centroids:
    43	            if abs(j[0] - cj[0]) <= tol and abs(j[1] - cj[1]) <= tol:
    44	                hit = cj
    45	                break
    46	        if hit is None:
    47	            centroids.append(j)
    48	            consensus[j] = j
    49	        else:
    50	            consensus[j] = hit
    51	    return consensus
    52	
    53	
    54	def build_graph(cons_chains: List[Chain]) -> Tuple[Counter, Counter]:
    55	    """Node + edge read-support counts from consensus-mapped chains.
    56	    Edge (a, b) = a and b are consecutive junctions in a read's chain."""
    57	    node_counts: Counter = Counter()
    58	    edge_counts: Counter = Counter()
    59	    for ch in cons_chains:
    60	        for j in ch:
    61	            node_counts[j] += 1
    62	        for a, b in zip(ch, ch[1:]):
    63	            edge_counts[(a, b)] += 1
    64	    return node_counts, edge_counts
    65	
    66	
    67	def anchored_5p_junctions(
    68	    cons_chains: List[Chain],
    69	    five_prime_pos: List[int],
    70	    strand: str,
    71	    tss_tol: int,
    72	    min_tss_reads: int,
    73	    tss_frac: float,
    74	) -> Set[Junction]:
    75	    """Return the set of 5'-terminal consensus junctions that sit at a real TSS.
    76	
    77	    dRNA is 3'-biased: a truncated read of a LONG transcript stops at a RANDOM 5'
    78	    position, but a COMPLETE read of a genuine short isoform stops at that
    79	    isoform's real TSS. So among the reads whose 5'-most junction is ``J`` (they
    80	    begin at ``J``), if a strong fraction pile their read-5'-ends within
    81	    ``tss_tol`` bp of one position, ``J`` marks a real transcription start and a
    82	    chain ending there must NOT be extended further 5'-ward (that would erase the
    83	    short isoform). Empirically (p00) such peaks carry 40-90% of a locus's reads;
    84	    degradation-only starts scatter and never cross ``tss_frac``.
    85	
    86	    5'-most junction is ``chain[0]`` on '+' (5' = small coord) and ``chain[-1]``
    87	    on '-' (5' = large coord).
    88	    """
    89	    by_term: Dict[Junction, List[int]] = defaultdict(list)
    90	    for cc, p in zip(cons_chains, five_prime_pos):
    91	        if not cc or p is None:
    92	            continue
    93	        term = cc[0] if strand == "+" else cc[-1]
    94	        by_term[term].append(p)
    95	    anchored: Set[Junction] = set()
    96	    for term, positions in by_term.items():
    97	        if len(positions) < min_tss_reads:
    98	            continue
    99	        best = max(sum(1 for q in positions if abs(q - p) <= tss_tol)
   100	                   for p in positions)
   101	        if best >= min_tss_reads and best >= tss_frac * len(positions):
   102	            anchored.add(term)
   103	    return anchored
   104	
   105	
   106	def assemble_chains(
   107	    chains: List[Chain],
   108	    tol: int,
   109	    min_edge_reads: int,
   110	    five_prime_pos: List[int] = None,
   111	    strand: str = "+",
   112	    tss_tol: int = 20,
   113	    min_tss_reads: int = 3,
   114	    tss_frac: float = 0.4,
   115	) -> Tuple[List[Chain], Dict[int, Chain]]:
   116	    """Assemble maximal chains by unambiguous extension.
   117	
   118	    Returns ``(cons_chains, extended_by_index)`` where ``cons_chains[i]`` is input
   119	    chain ``i`` mapped to consensus junctions, and ``extended_by_index[i]`` is the
   120	    maximal chain that chain ``i`` extends into (following only edges from a node
   121	    that has exactly ONE supported successor / predecessor). Two input reads whose
   122	    (possibly truncated) chains lie on the same maximal path map to the SAME
   123	    extended chain, so downstream grouping by the extended chain merges them.
   124	
   125	    5'-TSS brake: if ``five_prime_pos`` (per-input-chain read-5'-end genomic
   126	    positions) is given, a chain whose 5'-terminal junction is a real TSS
   127	    (:func:`anchored_5p_junctions`) is NOT extended in the 5' direction, so
   128	    genuine short isoforms contained inside a longer transcript survive instead of
   129	    being merged away. Without ``five_prime_pos`` behaviour is byte-identical to
   130	    the un-braked assembly (default-off contract).
   131	    """
   132	    cons = cluster_junctions(chains, tol)
   133	    cons_chains: List[Chain] = [tuple(cons[j] for j in ch) for ch in chains]
   134	    _, edge_counts = build_graph(cons_chains)
   135	
   136	    succ: Dict[Junction, List[Junction]] = defaultdict(list)
   137	    pred: Dict[Junction, List[Junction]] = defaultdict(list)
   138	    for (a, b), n in edge_counts.items():
   139	        if n >= min_edge_reads:
   140	            succ[a].append(b)
   141	            pred[b].append(a)
   142	
   143	    anchored: Set[Junction] = set()
   144	    if five_prime_pos is not None:
   145	        anchored = anchored_5p_junctions(
   146	            cons_chains, five_prime_pos, strand, tss_tol, min_tss_reads, tss_frac
   147	        )
   148	
   149	    def unique_succ(j: Junction):
   150	        s = succ.get(j)
   151	        return s[0] if s and len(s) == 1 else None
   152	
   153	    def unique_pred(j: Junction):
   154	        p = pred.get(j)
   155	        return p[0] if p and len(p) == 1 else None
   156	
   157	    cache: Dict[Chain, Chain] = {}
   158	
   159	    def extend(cc: Chain) -> Chain:
   160	        if cc in cache:
   161	            return cache[cc]
   162	        if not cc:
   163	            cache[cc] = cc
   164	            return cc
   165	        chain = list(cc)
   166	        seen: Set[Junction] = set(chain)
   167	        # 5' terminus of THIS read's chain (fixed; 3'-ward extension won't move it)
   168	        five_term = cc[0] if strand == "+" else cc[-1]
   169	        braked = five_term in anchored
   170	        # right extension is 5'-ward on '-' strand, 3'-ward on '+'
   171	        block_right = braked and strand == "-"
   172	        # left extension is 5'-ward on '+' strand, 3'-ward on '-'
   173	        block_left = braked and strand == "+"
   174	        while not block_right:
   175	            nxt = unique_succ(chain[-1])
   176	            if nxt is None or nxt in seen:
   177	                break
   178	            chain.append(nxt)
   179	            seen.add(nxt)
   180	        while not block_left:
   181	            prv = unique_pred(chain[0])
   182	            if prv is None or prv in seen:
   183	                break
   184	            chain.insert(0, prv)
   185	            seen.add(prv)
   186	        out = tuple(chain)
   187	        cache[cc] = out
   188	        return out
   189	
   190	    extended_by_index: Dict[int, Chain] = {
   191	        i: extend(cc) for i, cc in enumerate(cons_chains)
   192	    }
   193	    return cons_chains, extended_by_index
--- snap body ---
   193	def snap_junctions(
   194	    read_chains: List[Tuple[Dict, IntronChain]],
   195	    genome_seq: str,
   196	    strand: str,
   197	    snap_bp: int = 6,
   198	    min_support: int = 2,
   199	) -> List[Tuple[Dict, IntronChain]]:
   200	    """Snap noisy splice-site coordinates onto per-cluster consensus positions.
   201	
   202	    Algorithm:
   203	      1. Pool every read's donor (intron start) and acceptor (intron end)
   204	         positions independently.
   205	      2. Greedy 1D-cluster each pool with ``snap_bp`` as the join gap.
   206	      3. Pick a consensus per cluster, preferring canonical splice motifs
   207	         (see :func:`_pick_consensus`).
   208	      4. Rewrite each read's chain to the consensus coordinates. Clusters
   209	         with fewer than ``min_support`` reads keep their original positions
   210	         to avoid over-snapping rare junctions.
   211	      5. Drop any rewritten intron that becomes degenerate (``s >= e``).
   212	
   213	    The function does not mutate the input read dicts; it returns a new list
   214	    of ``(read_dict, IntronChain)`` tuples with replaced (or unchanged) chains.
   215	
   216	    Args:
   217	        read_chains: Per-read ``(read_dict, IntronChain)`` pairs.
   218	        genome_seq: Full chromosome sequence string used for motif lookup.
   219	        strand: "+" or "-".
   220	        snap_bp: Maximum within-cluster gap. Must be > 0 to do any work.
   221	        min_support: Minimum number of reads in a cluster before it is
   222	            snapped to its consensus.
   223	
   224	    Returns:
   225	        A list parallel to ``read_chains`` with (possibly) rewritten chains.
   226	    """
   227	    if snap_bp <= 0 or not read_chains:
   228	        return list(read_chains)
   229	
   230	    donors: List[int] = []
   231	    acceptors: List[int] = []
   232	    for _rd, chain in read_chains:
   233	        for s, e in chain.introns:
   234	            donor_pos = s if strand == "+" else e
   235	            acceptor_pos = e if strand == "+" else s
   236	            donors.append(donor_pos)
   237	            acceptors.append(acceptor_pos)
   238	
   239	    donor_clusters = _cluster_positions(donors, snap_bp)
   240	    acceptor_clusters = _cluster_positions(acceptors, snap_bp)
   241	
   242	    donor_map: Dict[int, int] = {}
   243	    for cluster in donor_clusters:
   244	        if len(cluster) < min_support:
   245	            for p in cluster:
   246	                donor_map[p] = p
   247	            continue
   248	        consensus = _pick_consensus(cluster, "donor", strand, genome_seq)
   249	        for p in cluster:
   250	            donor_map[p] = consensus
   251	
   252	    acceptor_map: Dict[int, int] = {}
   253	    for cluster in acceptor_clusters:
   254	        if len(cluster) < min_support:
   255	            for p in cluster:
   256	                acceptor_map[p] = p
   257	            continue
   258	        consensus = _pick_consensus(cluster, "acceptor", strand, genome_seq)
   259	        for p in cluster:
   260	            acceptor_map[p] = consensus
   261	
   262	    snapped: List[Tuple[Dict, IntronChain]] = []
   263	    for rd, chain in read_chains:
   264	        new_introns: List[Tuple[int, int]] = []
   265	        for s, e in chain.introns:
   266	            if strand == "+":
   267	                new_s = donor_map.get(s, s)
   268	                new_e = acceptor_map.get(e, e)
   269	            else:
   270	                new_s = acceptor_map.get(s, s)
   271	                new_e = donor_map.get(e, e)
   272	            if new_e > new_s:
   273	                new_introns.append((new_s, new_e))
   274	            # else: degenerate after snap, drop the intron
   275	        snapped.append((rd, IntronChain(introns=tuple(new_introns))))
   276	
   277	    return snapped

   395	    # Shadow-ratio guard for the wobble merge: absorb a wobble-matching novel
   396	    # candidate into a rep ONLY if it is a true SHADOW — len(cand.reads) <=
   397	    # ratio * len(rep.reads). Protects genuine CLOSE isoforms (real alt
   398	    # donor/acceptor a few bp apart, e.g. NAGNAG) from being merged: a minimap
   399	    # shadow has few reads, a real close isoform has comparable support. 1.0
   400	    # merges any wobble-match (naive; measured to eat correct transcripts at
   401	    # tol=6). isoquant's ½-support bulge criterion ≈ 0.5. Only used when
   402	    # denovo_wobble_tol > 0.

     1	"""De-novo intron graph: assemble full-length transcript intron chains from
     2	partial/truncated read chains.
     3	
     4	Measured motivation (p00, no annotation): pyfin's dominant de-novo error is
     5	truncation — candidates that are a contiguous sub-chain of a real transcript
     6	(gffcompare class 'c', ~16% of output), because dRNA reads are 3'-biased so a
     7	read covers only the 3' junctions. pyfin currently emits each read's exact chain
     8	as a candidate, so a truncated read -> a truncated candidate.
     9	
    10	This module pools all reads' junctions, clusters wobbled junctions to a
    11	read-count consensus, builds a directed read-adjacency graph (nodes = consensus
    12	junctions ordered by genomic coord; edges = "these two junctions are consecutive
    13	in >= min_edge_reads reads"), and EXTENDS each read's chain through
    14	UNAMBIGUOUSLY-supported edges to a maximal chain. Extension stops at genuine
    15	branch points (a node with >1 supported successor), so it lengthens truncated
    16	chains WITHOUT fabricating wrong junction combinations.
    17	
    18	NOT annotation-snap: consensus coordinates and edges come only from reads.
    19	"""
    20	from __future__ import annotations
    21	
    22	from collections import Counter, defaultdict
    23	from typing import Dict, List, Set, Tuple
    24	
    25	Junction = Tuple[int, int]
    26	Chain = Tuple[Junction, ...]
    27	
    28	
    29	def cluster_junctions(chains: List[Chain], tol: int) -> Dict[Junction, Junction]:
    30	    """Map every observed junction to a consensus junction. Greedy by read count:
    31	    the most-observed junction seeds a centroid; a junction within ``tol`` bp on
    32	    BOTH donor and acceptor maps to the first matching centroid. Deterministic
    33	    (count desc, then coordinate)."""
    34	    counts: Counter = Counter()
    35	    for ch in chains:
    36	        for j in ch:
    37	            counts[j] += 1
    38	    consensus: Dict[Junction, Junction] = {}
    39	    centroids: List[Junction] = []
    40	    for j in sorted(counts, key=lambda x: (-counts[x], x)):
    41	        hit = None
    42	        for cj in centroids:
    43	            if abs(j[0] - cj[0]) <= tol and abs(j[1] - cj[1]) <= tol:
    44	                hit = cj
    45	                break
    46	        if hit is None:
    47	            centroids.append(j)
    48	            consensus[j] = j
    49	        else:
    50	            consensus[j] = hit
    51	    return consensus
    52	
    53	
    54	def build_graph(cons_chains: List[Chain]) -> Tuple[Counter, Counter]:
    55	    """Node + edge read-support counts from consensus-mapped chains.
    56	    Edge (a, b) = a and b are consecutive junctions in a read's chain."""
    57	    node_counts: Counter = Counter()
    58	    edge_counts: Counter = Counter()
    59	    for ch in cons_chains:
    60	        for j in ch:
    61	            node_counts[j] += 1
    62	        for a, b in zip(ch, ch[1:]):
    63	            edge_counts[(a, b)] += 1
    64	    return node_counts, edge_counts
    65	
    66	
    67	def anchored_5p_junctions(
    68	    cons_chains: List[Chain],
    69	    five_prime_pos: List[int],
    70	    strand: str,
    71	    tss_tol: int,
    72	    min_tss_reads: int,
    73	    tss_frac: float,
    74	) -> Set[Junction]:
    75	    """Return the set of 5'-terminal consensus junctions that sit at a real TSS.
    76	
    77	    dRNA is 3'-biased: a truncated read of a LONG transcript stops at a RANDOM 5'
    78	    position, but a COMPLETE read of a genuine short isoform stops at that
    79	    isoform's real TSS. So among the reads whose 5'-most junction is ``J`` (they
    80	    begin at ``J``), if a strong fraction pile their read-5'-ends within
    81	    ``tss_tol`` bp of one position, ``J`` marks a real transcription start and a
    82	    chain ending there must NOT be extended further 5'-ward (that would erase the
    83	    short isoform). Empirically (p00) such peaks carry 40-90% of a locus's reads;
    84	    degradation-only starts scatter and never cross ``tss_frac``.
    85	
    86	    5'-most junction is ``chain[0]`` on '+' (5' = small coord) and ``chain[-1]``
    87	    on '-' (5' = large coord).
    88	    """
    89	    by_term: Dict[Junction, List[int]] = defaultdict(list)
    90	    for cc, p in zip(cons_chains, five_prime_pos):
    91	        if not cc or p is None:
    92	            continue
    93	        term = cc[0] if strand == "+" else cc[-1]
    94	        by_term[term].append(p)
    95	    anchored: Set[Junction] = set()
    96	    for term, positions in by_term.items():
    97	        if len(positions) < min_tss_reads:
    98	            continue
    99	        best = max(sum(1 for q in positions if abs(q - p) <= tss_tol)
   100	                   for p in positions)
   101	        if best >= min_tss_reads and best >= tss_frac * len(positions):
   102	            anchored.add(term)
   103	    return anchored
   104	
   105	
   106	def assemble_chains(
   107	    chains: List[Chain],
   108	    tol: int,
   109	    min_edge_reads: int,
   110	    five_prime_pos: List[int] = None,
   111	    strand: str = "+",
   112	    tss_tol: int = 20,
   113	    min_tss_reads: int = 3,
   114	    tss_frac: float = 0.4,
   115	) -> Tuple[List[Chain], Dict[int, Chain]]:
   116	    """Assemble maximal chains by unambiguous extension.
   117	
   118	    Returns ``(cons_chains, extended_by_index)`` where ``cons_chains[i]`` is input
   119	    chain ``i`` mapped to consensus junctions, and ``extended_by_index[i]`` is the
   120	    maximal chain that chain ``i`` extends into (following only edges from a node
   121	    that has exactly ONE supported successor / predecessor). Two input reads whose
   122	    (possibly truncated) chains lie on the same maximal path map to the SAME
   123	    extended chain, so downstream grouping by the extended chain merges them.
   124	
   125	    5'-TSS brake: if ``five_prime_pos`` (per-input-chain read-5'-end genomic
   126	    positions) is given, a chain whose 5'-terminal junction is a real TSS
   127	    (:func:`anchored_5p_junctions`) is NOT extended in the 5' direction, so
   128	    genuine short isoforms contained inside a longer transcript survive instead of
   129	    being merged away. Without ``five_prime_pos`` behaviour is byte-identical to
   130	    the un-braked assembly (default-off contract).
   131	    """
   132	    cons = cluster_junctions(chains, tol)
   133	    cons_chains: List[Chain] = [tuple(cons[j] for j in ch) for ch in chains]
   134	    _, edge_counts = build_graph(cons_chains)
   135	
   136	    succ: Dict[Junction, List[Junction]] = defaultdict(list)
   137	    pred: Dict[Junction, List[Junction]] = defaultdict(list)
   138	    for (a, b), n in edge_counts.items():
   139	        if n >= min_edge_reads:
   140	            succ[a].append(b)
   141	            pred[b].append(a)
   142	
   143	    anchored: Set[Junction] = set()
   144	    if five_prime_pos is not None:
   145	        anchored = anchored_5p_junctions(
   146	            cons_chains, five_prime_pos, strand, tss_tol, min_tss_reads, tss_frac
   147	        )
   148	
   149	    def unique_succ(j: Junction):
   150	        s = succ.get(j)
   151	        return s[0] if s and len(s) == 1 else None
   152	
   153	    def unique_pred(j: Junction):
   154	        p = pred.get(j)
   155	        return p[0] if p and len(p) == 1 else None
   156	
   157	    cache: Dict[Chain, Chain] = {}
   158	
   159	    def extend(cc: Chain) -> Chain:
   160	        if cc in cache:
   161	            return cache[cc]
   162	        if not cc:
   163	            cache[cc] = cc
   164	            return cc
   165	        chain = list(cc)
   166	        seen: Set[Junction] = set(chain)
   167	        # 5' terminus of THIS read's chain (fixed; 3'-ward extension won't move it)
   168	        five_term = cc[0] if strand == "+" else cc[-1]
   169	        braked = five_term in anchored
   170	        # right extension is 5'-ward on '-' strand, 3'-ward on '+'
   171	        block_right = braked and strand == "-"
   172	        # left extension is 5'-ward on '+' strand, 3'-ward on '-'
   173	        block_left = braked and strand == "+"
   174	        while not block_right:
   175	            nxt = unique_succ(chain[-1])
   176	            if nxt is None or nxt in seen:
   177	                break
   178	            chain.append(nxt)
   179	            seen.add(nxt)
   180	        while not block_left:
   181	            prv = unique_pred(chain[0])
   182	            if prv is None or prv in seen:
   183	                break
   184	            chain.insert(0, prv)
   185	            seen.add(prv)
   186	        out = tuple(chain)
   187	        cache[cc] = out
   188	        return out
   189	
   190	    extended_by_index: Dict[int, Chain] = {
   191	        i: extend(cc) for i, cc in enumerate(cons_chains)
   192	    }
   193	    return cons_chains, extended_by_index
--- snap 193-277 ---
   193	def snap_junctions(
   194	    read_chains: List[Tuple[Dict, IntronChain]],
   195	    genome_seq: str,
   196	    strand: str,
   197	    snap_bp: int = 6,
   198	    min_support: int = 2,
   199	) -> List[Tuple[Dict, IntronChain]]:
   200	    """Snap noisy splice-site coordinates onto per-cluster consensus positions.
   201	
   202	    Algorithm:
   203	      1. Pool every read's donor (intron start) and acceptor (intron end)
   204	         positions independently.
   205	      2. Greedy 1D-cluster each pool with ``snap_bp`` as the join gap.
   206	      3. Pick a consensus per cluster, preferring canonical splice motifs
   207	         (see :func:`_pick_consensus`).
   208	      4. Rewrite each read's chain to the consensus coordinates. Clusters
   209	         with fewer than ``min_support`` reads keep their original positions
   210	         to avoid over-snapping rare junctions.
   211	      5. Drop any rewritten intron that becomes degenerate (``s >= e``).
   212	
   213	    The function does not mutate the input read dicts; it returns a new list
   214	    of ``(read_dict, IntronChain)`` tuples with replaced (or unchanged) chains.
   215	
   216	    Args:
   217	        read_chains: Per-read ``(read_dict, IntronChain)`` pairs.
   218	        genome_seq: Full chromosome sequence string used for motif lookup.
   219	        strand: "+" or "-".
   220	        snap_bp: Maximum within-cluster gap. Must be > 0 to do any work.
   221	        min_support: Minimum number of reads in a cluster before it is
   222	            snapped to its consensus.
   223	
   224	    Returns:
   225	        A list parallel to ``read_chains`` with (possibly) rewritten chains.
   226	    """
   227	    if snap_bp <= 0 or not read_chains:
   228	        return list(read_chains)
   229	
   230	    donors: List[int] = []
   231	    acceptors: List[int] = []
   232	    for _rd, chain in read_chains:
   233	        for s, e in chain.introns:
   234	            donor_pos = s if strand == "+" else e
   235	            acceptor_pos = e if strand == "+" else s
   236	            donors.append(donor_pos)
   237	            acceptors.append(acceptor_pos)
   238	
   239	    donor_clusters = _cluster_positions(donors, snap_bp)
   240	    acceptor_clusters = _cluster_positions(acceptors, snap_bp)
   241	
   242	    donor_map: Dict[int, int] = {}
   243	    for cluster in donor_clusters:
   244	        if len(cluster) < min_support:
   245	            for p in cluster:
   246	                donor_map[p] = p
   247	            continue
   248	        consensus = _pick_consensus(cluster, "donor", strand, genome_seq)
   249	        for p in cluster:
   250	            donor_map[p] = consensus
   251	
   252	    acceptor_map: Dict[int, int] = {}
   253	    for cluster in acceptor_clusters:
   254	        if len(cluster) < min_support:
   255	            for p in cluster:
   256	                acceptor_map[p] = p
   257	            continue
   258	        consensus = _pick_consensus(cluster, "acceptor", strand, genome_seq)
   259	        for p in cluster:
   260	            acceptor_map[p] = consensus
   261	
   262	    snapped: List[Tuple[Dict, IntronChain]] = []
   263	    for rd, chain in read_chains:
   264	        new_introns: List[Tuple[int, int]] = []
   265	        for s, e in chain.introns:
   266	            if strand == "+":
   267	                new_s = donor_map.get(s, s)
   268	                new_e = acceptor_map.get(e, e)
   269	            else:
   270	                new_s = acceptor_map.get(s, s)
   271	                new_e = donor_map.get(e, e)
   272	            if new_e > new_s:
   273	                new_introns.append((new_s, new_e))
   274	            # else: degenerate after snap, drop the intron
   275	        snapped.append((rd, IntronChain(introns=tuple(new_introns))))
   276	
   277	    return snapped
--- grouping exact ---
   486	def group_reads_by_three_prime_and_intron_chain(
   487	    read_dicts: List[Dict],
   488	    strand: str,
   489	    threshold: int = 24,
   490	    chain_override: Optional[Dict[str, IntronChain]] = None,
   491	    multi_chain_override: Optional[Dict[str, List[IntronChain]]] = None,
   492	) -> Dict[Tuple[int, IntronChain], List[Dict]]:
   493	    """Group reads by clustered 3' end position and intron chain.
   494	
   495	    1. Extract 3' position and intron chain per read.
   496	    2. Sort by 3' position.
   497	    3. Cluster 3' positions within *threshold* bp.
   498	    4. Sub-group each cluster by intron chain.
   499	    5. Use consensus (median) 3' position per cluster.
   500	
   501	    Args:
   502	        read_dicts: List of alignment dicts from BamReader.alignment_to_dict().
   503	        strand: '+' or '-'.
   504	        threshold: Maximum distance between 3' ends in a cluster.
   505	        chain_override: Optional mapping ``query_name -> IntronChain`` that
   506	            replaces the CIGAR-derived chain (used for junction-snap output).
   507	        multi_chain_override: Optional mapping ``query_name -> [IntronChain, ...]``
   508	            that produces multiple grouping entries per read (used for the
   509	            canonical-motif alternative expansion). Takes precedence over
   510	            ``chain_override`` when both are provided for the same read.
   511	
   512	    Returns:
   513	        Dict mapping (consensus_3prime, IntronChain) -> list of read dicts.
   514	    """
   515	    # Annotate each read with its 3' pos and intron chain
   516	    annotated: List[Tuple[int, IntronChain, Dict]] = []
   517	    for rd in read_dicts:
   518	        three_prime = extract_three_prime_pos(rd)
   519	        if three_prime is None:
   520	            continue
   521	        cigar = rd.get("cigartuples")
   522	        ref_start = rd.get("reference_start")
   523	        if cigar is None or ref_start is None:
   524	            continue
   525	        qname = rd.get("query_name")
   526	        chains_for_this_read: Optional[List[IntronChain]] = None
   527	        if multi_chain_override is not None and qname is not None \
   528	                and qname in multi_chain_override:
   529	            chains_for_this_read = multi_chain_override[qname]
   530	        elif chain_override is not None and qname is not None \
   531	                and qname in chain_override:
   532	            chains_for_this_read = [chain_override[qname]]
   533	        if chains_for_this_read is None:
   534	            chains_for_this_read = [extract_intron_chain(cigar, ref_start)]
   535	        for chain in chains_for_this_read:
   536	            annotated.append((three_prime, chain, rd))
   537	
   538	    if not annotated:
   539	        return {}
   540	
   541	    # Sort by 3' position
   542	    annotated.sort(key=lambda x: x[0])
   543	
   544	    # Cluster by 3' proximity
   545	    clusters: List[List[Tuple[int, IntronChain, Dict]]] = []
   546	    current_cluster: List[Tuple[int, IntronChain, Dict]] = [annotated[0]]
   547	
   548	    for item in annotated[1:]:
   549	        if item[0] - current_cluster[-1][0] <= threshold:
   550	            current_cluster.append(item)
   551	        else:
   552	            clusters.append(current_cluster)
   553	            current_cluster = [item]
   554	    clusters.append(current_cluster)
   555	
   556	    # Sub-group each cluster by intron chain, compute consensus 3'
   557	    groups: Dict[Tuple[int, IntronChain], List[Dict]] = {}
   558	    for cluster in clusters:
   559	        # Consensus 3' = median of cluster positions
   560	        positions = [x[0] for x in cluster]
   561	        consensus_3prime = sorted(positions)[len(positions) // 2]
   562	
   563	        by_chain: Dict[IntronChain, List[Dict]] = defaultdict(list)
   564	        for _, chain, rd in cluster:
   565	            by_chain[chain].append(rd)
   566	
   567	        for chain, reads in by_chain.items():
   568	            groups[(consensus_3prime, chain)] = reads
   569	
   570	    return groups
   571	
   572

   352	        # Save the last cluster
   353	        if current_cluster:
   354	            clustered.append(GenomicInterval(
   355	                chrom=chrom,
   356	                start=current_cluster[0],
   357	                end=current_cluster[1],
   358	                strand=strand,
   359	                read_count=len(current_reads),
   360	                interval_id=f"interval_{interval_counter:06d}",
   361	                attrs=current_reads,
   362	                three_prime_pos=current_three_prime_pos
   363	            ))
   364	            interval_counter += 1
   365	
   366	    logger.info(f"Created {len(clustered)} clustered intervals (strand-separated)")
   367	    return clustered
   368	
   369	
   370	def generate_intervals_from_gtf(gtf_path: str) -> List[GenomicInterval]:
--- merge intervals strand ---
   403	def merge_gtf_and_read_intervals(gtf_intervals: List[GenomicInterval],
   404	                                read_intervals: List[GenomicInterval],
   405	                                max_gap: int = 50) -> List[GenomicInterval]:
   406	    """
   407	    Merge GTF annotation intervals with read coverage intervals
   408	
   409	    Args:
   410	        gtf_intervals: Intervals from GTF
   411	        read_intervals: Intervals from BAM reads (clustered)
   412	        max_gap: Maximum gap for merging intervals
   413	
   414	    Returns:
   415	        List of merged, non-overlapping GenomicInterval objects (strand-separated)
   416	    """
   417	    logger.info("Merging GTF intervals with read coverage...")
   418	
   419	    # Combine all intervals
   420	    all_intervals = gtf_intervals + read_intervals
   421	
   422	    # Group by chromosome and strand
   423	    chrom_strand_intervals = defaultdict(list)
   424	    for interval in all_intervals:
   425	        key = (interval.chrom, interval.strand)
   426	        chrom_strand_intervals[key].append(interval)
   427	
   428	    # Merge intervals on each chromosome and strand
   429	    merged = []
   430	
   431	    # Sort with proper handling of None strand values
   432	    # Convert None to a sortable value (use 'z' so it sorts after '+' and '-')
   433	    for (chrom, strand), intervals in sorted(
   434	        chrom_strand_intervals.items(),
   435	        key=lambda x: (x[0][0], 'z' if x[0][1] is None else x[0][1])
   436	    ):
   437	        if not intervals:
   438	            continue
   439	
   440	        # Sort by start position
   441	        intervals_sorted = sorted(intervals, key=lambda x: x.start)
   442	
   443	        merged_chrom = []
   444	        current = intervals_sorted[0]
   445	
   446	        for interval in intervals_sorted[1:]:
   447	            # Check if intervals should be merged (same strand check already done)
   448	            if (current.overlaps(interval) or interval.start <= current.end + max_gap):
   449	                # Merge intervals
   450	                try:
   451	                    current = current.merge(interval)
   452	                except ValueError:
   453	                    # Different strand (shouldn't happen with our grouping)
   454	                    merged_chrom.append(current)
   455	                    current = interval
   456	            else:
   457	                # No overlap, save current
   458	                merged_chrom.append(current)
   459	                current = interval
   460	
   461	        # Save the last interval
   462	        merged_chrom.append(current)
   463	        merged.extend(merged_chrom)
   464	
   465	    # Drop intervals with no reads (GTF-only intervals that no BAM read covers).
   466	    before = len(merged)
   467	    merged = [iv for iv in merged if iv.read_count > 0]
   468	    dropped = before - len(merged)
   469	    if dropped:
   470	        logger.info(f"Dropped {dropped} intervals with read_count==0 (no BAM coverage)")
   471	
   472	    logger.info(f"Generated {len(merged)} final intervals (strand-separated)")
   473	    return merged
   474	
   475	
--- isolated pipeline ---
   476	def generate_isolated_intervals(bam_path: str,
   477	                                gtf_path: Optional[str] = None,
   478	                                max_gap: int =0,
   479	                                max_reads: Optional[int] = None, tmp_dir: Optional[str] = None) -> Dict[str, Any]:
   480	    """
   481	    Generate isolated, non-overlapping genomic intervals with strand separation
   482	
   483	    This is the main function that generates intervals by analyzing both
   484	    GTF annotation and BAM read alignments. Fusion reads are identified
   485	    and skipped during interval generation.
   486	
   487	    Args:
   488	        bam_path: Path to BAM file (required)
   489	        gtf_path: Path to GTF file (optional, improves annotation)
   490	        max_gap: Maximum gap between intervals to merge (default: 0)
   491	        max_reads: Optional limit on number of reads to process
   492	
   493	    Returns:
   494	        Dictionary with:
   495	        - 'intervals': List of GenomicInterval objects (strand-separated)
   496	        - 'fusion_read_ids': Set of fusion read IDs
   497	        - 'fusion_reads': List of full read dicts for fusion reads
   498	        - 'num_reads_processed': Number of reads processed
   499	    """
   500	    logger.info("=" * 60)
   501	    logger.info("Starting interval generation")
   502	    logger.info(f"BAM: {bam_path}")
   503	    logger.info(f"GTF: {gtf_path}")
   504	    logger.info(f"Max gap: {max_gap}bp")
   505	    logger.info("=" * 60)
   506	
   507	    # Process BAM reads
   508	    read_alignments, fusion_read_ids, fusion_reads = generate_intervals_from_reads(bam_path, max_reads)
   509	
   510	    # Cluster read alignments
   511	    read_intervals = cluster_intervals(read_alignments, max_gap)
   512	    # logger.debug(read_intervals)
   513	    if tmp_dir:
   514	        Path(tmp_dir).mkdir(exist_ok=True)
   515	        tmp_read_intervals = Path(tmp_dir) / "read_intervals.bed"
   516	        # tmp_read_intervals.mkdir(exist_ok=True)
   517	        intervals_to_bed(read_intervals,tmp_read_intervals)
   518	    # Generate intervals from GTF if provided
   519	    gtf_intervals = []
   520	    if gtf_path:
   521	        gtf_intervals = generate_intervals_from_gtf(gtf_path)
   522	    if tmp_dir:
   523	        tmp_gtf_intervals = Path(tmp_dir) / "gtf_intervals.bed"
   524	        # tmp_gtf_intervals.mkdir(exist_ok=True)
   525	        intervals_to_bed(gtf_intervals,tmp_gtf_intervals)
   526	    # Merge intervals
   527	    final_intervals = merge_gtf_and_read_intervals(
   528	        gtf_intervals,
   529	        read_intervals,
   530	        max_gap=max_gap
   531	    )
   532	    # logger.debug(final_intervals)
   533	    # Sort intervals by chromosome, strand, position
   534	    strand_order = {'+': 0, '-': 1, None: 2}
   535	    final_intervals.sort(key=lambda x: (x.chrom, strand_order.get(x.strand, 2), x.start))
   536	    if tmp_dir:
   537	        tmp_final_intervals = Path(tmp_dir) / "final_intervals.bed"
   538	        # tmp_final_intervals.mkdir(exist_ok=True)
   539	        intervals_to_bed(final_intervals,tmp_final_intervals)
   540	    # Log summary
   541	    logger.info("=" * 60)
   542	    logger.info("Interval generation completed")
   543	    logger.info(f"Total intervals: {len(final_intervals)}")
   544	    logger.info(f"Fusion reads detected: {len(fusion_read_ids)}")
   545	
   546	    # Log intervals by strand
   547	    strand_counts = defaultdict(int)
   548	    for interval in final_intervals:
   549	        strand_counts[interval.strand] += 1
   550	
   551	    
   552	    logger.info("Intervals by strand:")
   553	    logger.debug(strand_counts.items())
   554	    for strand, count in sorted(strand_counts.items()):
   555	        logger.info(f"  {strand if strand else 'unstranded'}: {count}")
   556	
   557	    logger.info("=" * 60)
   558	
   559	    # Log first 5 intervals
   560	    logger.info("First 5 intervals:")
   561	    for i, interval in enumerate(final_intervals[:5], 1):
   562	        logger.info(f"  {i}. {interval.region_string} [strand: {interval.strand}] "
   563	                   f"(reads: {interval.read_count})")
   564	
   565	    if len(final_intervals) > 5:
   566	        logger.info(f"  ... and {len(final_intervals) - 5} more intervals")
   567	
   568	    return {
   569	        'intervals': final_intervals,
   570	        'fusion_read_ids': fusion_read_ids,
   571	        'fusion_reads': fusion_reads,
   572	        'num_reads_processed': len(read_alignments) + len(fusion_read_ids)
   573	    }
   574	
   575

--- graph assemble 106-193 ---
   106	def assemble_chains(
   107	    chains: List[Chain],
   108	    tol: int,
   109	    min_edge_reads: int,
   110	    five_prime_pos: List[int] = None,
   111	    strand: str = "+",
   112	    tss_tol: int = 20,
   113	    min_tss_reads: int = 3,
   114	    tss_frac: float = 0.4,
   115	) -> Tuple[List[Chain], Dict[int, Chain]]:
   116	    """Assemble maximal chains by unambiguous extension.
   117	
   118	    Returns ``(cons_chains, extended_by_index)`` where ``cons_chains[i]`` is input
   119	    chain ``i`` mapped to consensus junctions, and ``extended_by_index[i]`` is the
   120	    maximal chain that chain ``i`` extends into (following only edges from a node
   121	    that has exactly ONE supported successor / predecessor). Two input reads whose
   122	    (possibly truncated) chains lie on the same maximal path map to the SAME
   123	    extended chain, so downstream grouping by the extended chain merges them.
   124	
   125	    5'-TSS brake: if ``five_prime_pos`` (per-input-chain read-5'-end genomic
   126	    positions) is given, a chain whose 5'-terminal junction is a real TSS
   127	    (:func:`anchored_5p_junctions`) is NOT extended in the 5' direction, so
   128	    genuine short isoforms contained inside a longer transcript survive instead of
   129	    being merged away. Without ``five_prime_pos`` behaviour is byte-identical to
   130	    the un-braked assembly (default-off contract).
   131	    """
   132	    cons = cluster_junctions(chains, tol)
   133	    cons_chains: List[Chain] = [tuple(cons[j] for j in ch) for ch in chains]
   134	    _, edge_counts = build_graph(cons_chains)
   135	
   136	    succ: Dict[Junction, List[Junction]] = defaultdict(list)
   137	    pred: Dict[Junction, List[Junction]] = defaultdict(list)
   138	    for (a, b), n in edge_counts.items():
   139	        if n >= min_edge_reads:
   140	            succ[a].append(b)
   141	            pred[b].append(a)
   142	
   143	    anchored: Set[Junction] = set()
   144	    if five_prime_pos is not None:
   145	        anchored = anchored_5p_junctions(
   146	            cons_chains, five_prime_pos, strand, tss_tol, min_tss_reads, tss_frac
   147	        )
   148	
   149	    def unique_succ(j: Junction):
   150	        s = succ.get(j)
   151	        return s[0] if s and len(s) == 1 else None
   152	
   153	    def unique_pred(j: Junction):
   154	        p = pred.get(j)
   155	        return p[0] if p and len(p) == 1 else None
   156	
   157	    cache: Dict[Chain, Chain] = {}
   158	
   159	    def extend(cc: Chain) -> Chain:
   160	        if cc in cache:
   161	            return cache[cc]
   162	        if not cc:
   163	            cache[cc] = cc
   164	            return cc
   165	        chain = list(cc)
   166	        seen: Set[Junction] = set(chain)
   167	        # 5' terminus of THIS read's chain (fixed; 3'-ward extension won't move it)
   168	        five_term = cc[0] if strand == "+" else cc[-1]
   169	        braked = five_term in anchored
   170	        # right extension is 5'-ward on '-' strand, 3'-ward on '+'
   171	        block_right = braked and strand == "-"
   172	        # left extension is 5'-ward on '+' strand, 3'-ward on '-'
   173	        block_left = braked and strand == "+"
   174	        while not block_right:
   175	            nxt = unique_succ(chain[-1])
   176	            if nxt is None or nxt in seen:
   177	                break
   178	            chain.append(nxt)
   179	            seen.add(nxt)
   180	        while not block_left:
   181	            prv = unique_pred(chain[0])
   182	            if prv is None or prv in seen:
   183	                break
   184	            chain.insert(0, prv)
   185	            seen.add(prv)
   186	        out = tuple(chain)
   187	        cache[cc] = out
   188	        return out
   189	
   190	    extended_by_index: Dict[int, Chain] = {
   191	        i: extend(cc) for i, cc in enumerate(cons_chains)
   192	    }
   193	    return cons_chains, extended_by_index
--- snap full body 193-277 ---
   193	def snap_junctions(
   194	    read_chains: List[Tuple[Dict, IntronChain]],
   195	    genome_seq: str,
   196	    strand: str,
   197	    snap_bp: int = 6,
   198	    min_support: int = 2,
   199	) -> List[Tuple[Dict, IntronChain]]:
   200	    """Snap noisy splice-site coordinates onto per-cluster consensus positions.
   201	
   202	    Algorithm:
   203	      1. Pool every read's donor (intron start) and acceptor (intron end)
   204	         positions independently.
   205	      2. Greedy 1D-cluster each pool with ``snap_bp`` as the join gap.
   206	      3. Pick a consensus per cluster, preferring canonical splice motifs
   207	         (see :func:`_pick_consensus`).
   208	      4. Rewrite each read's chain to the consensus coordinates. Clusters
   209	         with fewer than ``min_support`` reads keep their original positions
   210	         to avoid over-snapping rare junctions.
   211	      5. Drop any rewritten intron that becomes degenerate (``s >= e``).
   212	
   213	    The function does not mutate the input read dicts; it returns a new list
   214	    of ``(read_dict, IntronChain)`` tuples with replaced (or unchanged) chains.
   215	
   216	    Args:
   217	        read_chains: Per-read ``(read_dict, IntronChain)`` pairs.
   218	        genome_seq: Full chromosome sequence string used for motif lookup.
   219	        strand: "+" or "-".
   220	        snap_bp: Maximum within-cluster gap. Must be > 0 to do any work.
   221	        min_support: Minimum number of reads in a cluster before it is
   222	            snapped to its consensus.
   223	
   224	    Returns:
   225	        A list parallel to ``read_chains`` with (possibly) rewritten chains.
   226	    """
   227	    if snap_bp <= 0 or not read_chains:
   228	        return list(read_chains)
   229	
   230	    donors: List[int] = []
   231	    acceptors: List[int] = []
   232	    for _rd, chain in read_chains:
   233	        for s, e in chain.introns:
   234	            donor_pos = s if strand == "+" else e
   235	            acceptor_pos = e if strand == "+" else s
   236	            donors.append(donor_pos)
   237	            acceptors.append(acceptor_pos)
   238	
   239	    donor_clusters = _cluster_positions(donors, snap_bp)
   240	    acceptor_clusters = _cluster_positions(acceptors, snap_bp)
   241	
   242	    donor_map: Dict[int, int] = {}
   243	    for cluster in donor_clusters:
   244	        if len(cluster) < min_support:
   245	            for p in cluster:
   246	                donor_map[p] = p
   247	            continue
   248	        consensus = _pick_consensus(cluster, "donor", strand, genome_seq)
   249	        for p in cluster:
   250	            donor_map[p] = consensus
   251	
   252	    acceptor_map: Dict[int, int] = {}
   253	    for cluster in acceptor_clusters:
   254	        if len(cluster) < min_support:
   255	            for p in cluster:
   256	                acceptor_map[p] = p
   257	            continue
   258	        consensus = _pick_consensus(cluster, "acceptor", strand, genome_seq)
   259	        for p in cluster:
   260	            acceptor_map[p] = consensus
   261	
   262	    snapped: List[Tuple[Dict, IntronChain]] = []
   263	    for rd, chain in read_chains:
   264	        new_introns: List[Tuple[int, int]] = []
   265	        for s, e in chain.introns:
   266	            if strand == "+":
   267	                new_s = donor_map.get(s, s)
   268	                new_e = acceptor_map.get(e, e)
   269	            else:
   270	                new_s = acceptor_map.get(s, s)
   271	                new_e = donor_map.get(e, e)
   272	            if new_e > new_s:
   273	                new_introns.append((new_s, new_e))
   274	            # else: degenerate after snap, drop the intron
   275	        snapped.append((rd, IntronChain(introns=tuple(new_introns))))
   276	
   277	    return snapped

    36	        continue

    38	    if aln.reference_start <= FL_TOL and aln.reference_end >= L - FL_TOL:
    39	        fl[enst(tb.get_reference_name(aln.reference_id))] += 1
    40	fl3 = {t for t, v in fl.items() if v >= 3}
    41	
    42	# 2. matched (cf refmap class '=')
    43	matched = set()
    44	with open(CF_RM) as f:
    45	    next(f, None)
    46	    for ln in f:
    47	        c = ln.rstrip("\n").split("\t")
    48	        if len(c) >= 3 and c[2] == "=":
    49	            matched.add(c[1])
    50	
    51	missed_ids = fl3 - matched
    52	print(f"FL>=3={len(fl3)}  matched(of FL>=3)={len(fl3 & matched)}  MISSED={len(missed_ids)}", flush=True)
    53	
    54	# 3. parse truth GTF for ONLY the missed ids (fast: string ops + set lookup)
    55	tx = {}
    56	with open(TRUTH) as fh:
    57	    for line in fh:
    58	        if line[0] == "#":
    59	            continue
    60	        p = line.split("\t")
    61	        if len(p) < 9 or p[2] != "exon":
    62	            continue
    63	        a = p[8]
    64	        k = a.find('transcript_id "')
    65	        if k < 0:
    66	            continue
    67	        s = k + 15
    68	        tid = a[s:a.find('"', s)]
    69	        if tid not in missed_ids:
    70	            continue
    71	        t = tx.setdefault(tid, {"chrom": p[0], "strand": p[6], "exons": []})
    72	        t["exons"].append((int(p[3]) - 1, int(p[4])))
    73	gt = {}
    74	for tid, t in tx.items():
    75	    ex = sorted(t["exons"])
    76	    intr = tuple((ex[i][1], ex[i + 1][0]) for i in range(len(ex) - 1))
    77	    gt[tid] = {"chrom": t["chrom"], "strand": t["strand"], "chain": intr,
    78	               "start": ex[0][0], "end": ex[-1][1], "nexon": len(ex)}
    79	print(f"truth chains built for {len(gt)} missed transcripts", flush=True)
    80	
    81	
    82	def rchain(aln):
    83	    intr = []
    84	    pos = aln.reference_start
    85	    for op, ln in aln.cigartuples:
    86	        if op in (0, 7, 8, 2):
    87	            pos += ln
    88	        elif op == 3:
    89	            intr.append((pos, pos + ln))
    90	            pos += ln
    91	    return tuple(intr)
    92	
    93	
    94	def close(c1, c2):
    95	    if len(c1) != len(c2) or not c1:
    96	        return False
    97	    return all(abs(a - c) <= TOL and abs(b - d) <= TOL for (a, b), (c, d) in zip(c1, c2))
    98	
    99	
   100	def is_sub(short, long_):
   101	    n, m = len(long_), len(short)
   102	    if m == 0 or m >= n:
   103	        return False
   104	    return any(all(abs(short[k][0] - long_[o + k][0]) <= TOL and
   105	                   abs(short[k][1] - long_[o + k][1]) <= TOL for k in range(m))
   106	               for o in range(n - m + 1))
   107	
   108	
   109	gb = pysam.AlignmentFile(GBAM, "rb")
   110	cat = Counter()
   111	exon_by_cat = Counter()
   112	for t in sorted(gt):
   113	    g = gt[t]
   114	    tc = g["chain"]
   115	    if not tc:
   116	        cat["mono_truth"] += 1
   117	        continue
   118	    try:
   119	        it = gb.fetch(g["chrom"], max(g["start"], 0), g["end"])
   120	    except (ValueError, KeyError):
   121	        cat["E_no_genome_reads"] += 1
   122	        continue
   123	    exact = wob = sub = nreads = scanned = 0
   124	    for aln in it:
   125	        scanned += 1
   126	        if scanned > MAXREADS:
   127	            break
   128	        if aln.is_unmapped or aln.is_secondary or aln.is_supplementary:
   129	            continue
   130	        if ("-" if aln.is_reverse else "+") != g["strand"]:
   131	            continue
   132	        ct = aln.cigartuples
   133	        if not ct:
   134	            continue
   135	        if (ct[0][0] == 4 and ct[0][1] >= 250) or (ct[-1][0] == 4 and ct[-1][1] >= 250):
   136	            continue
   137	        rc = rchain(aln)
   138	        if not rc:
   139	            continue
   140	        nreads += 1
   141	        if rc == tc:
   142	            exact += 1
   143	            break
   144	        elif close(rc, tc):
   145	            wob += 1
   146	        elif is_sub(rc, tc):
   147	            sub += 1
   148	    key = ("A_has_EXACT_read" if exact else "B_wobble_only" if wob else
   149	           "C_subchain_only" if sub else "D_other_structure" if nreads else "E_no_genome_reads")
   150	    cat[key] += 1
   151	    b = "2-3ex" if g["nexon"] <= 3 else ("4-8ex" if g["nexon"] <= 8 else ">8ex")
   152	    exon_by_cat[(key, b)] += 1
   153	
   154	print(f"\n{'category':<22}{'count':>7}{'%':>7}")
   155	for k in ["A_has_EXACT_read", "B_wobble_only", "C_subchain_only",
   156	          "D_other_structure", "E_no_genome_reads", "mono_truth"]:
   157	    v = cat.get(k, 0)
   158	    print(f"{k:<22}{v:>7}{100 * v / max(len(missed_ids), 1):>6.1f}%")
   159	print("\nby exon-count:")
   160	for k in ["B_wobble_only", "C_subchain_only", "D_other_structure"]:
   161	    print(f"  {k:<20} " + " ".join(f"{b}={exon_by_cat.get((k, b), 0)}"
   162	                                   for b in ["2-3ex", "4-8ex", ">8ex"]))
--- compute FL ---
    25	    m = ENST.search(x)
    26	    return m.group(1) if m else x
    27	
    28	
    29	b = pysam.AlignmentFile(TBAM, "rb")
    30	reflen = b.lengths
    31	fl = Counter()
    32	tot = Counter()
    33	n = 0
    34	for aln in b.fetch(until_eof=True):
    35	    if aln.is_unmapped or aln.is_secondary or aln.is_supplementary:
    36	        continue
    37	    L = reflen[aln.reference_id]
    38	    tid = norm(b.get_reference_name(aln.reference_id))
    39	    tot[tid] += 1
    40	    if aln.reference_start <= FL_TOL and aln.reference_end >= L - FL_TOL:
    41	        fl[tid] += 1
    42	    n += 1
    43	print(f"primary reads={n}  transcripts_with_any_read={len(tot)}  "
    44	      f"with_FL>=1={sum(1 for v in fl.values() if v >= 1)}  "
    45	      f"with_FL>=3={sum(1 for v in fl.values() if v >= 3)}", flush=True)
    46	
    47	# truth transcript ids
    48	truth = set()
    49	for ln in open(TRUTH):
    50	    if ln.startswith("#"):
    51	        continue
    52	    p = ln.split("\t")
    53	    if len(p) < 9 or p[2] != "exon":
    54	        continue
    55	    m = re.search(r'transcript_id "([^"]+)"', p[8])
    56	    if m:
    57	        truth.add(m.group(1))
    58	
    59	
    60	def matched(rm):
    61	    s = set()
    62	    with open(rm) as f:
    63	        next(f, None)
    64	        for ln in f:
    65	            c = ln.rstrip("\n").split("\t")
    66	            if len(c) >= 3 and c[2] == "=":
    67	                s.add(c[1])
    68	    return s
    69	
    70	
    71	cf = matched(CF_RM)
    72	prod = matched(PROD_RM)
    73	fl_in_truth = {t: fl.get(t, 0) for t in truth}

    75	print(f"\ndenominator = TRUTH transcripts with >=k FULL-LENGTH reads (FL_TOL={FL_TOL}bp both ends)")
    76	print(f"cf output={CF_NOUT}  prod output={PROD_NOUT}\n")
    77	print(f"{'FL>=':>5}{'#truth':>9} | {'cf_Sn%':>7}{'cf_Pr%':>7} | {'prod_Sn%':>9}{'prod_Pr%':>9}")
    78	print("-" * 60)
    79	for k in [1, 2, 3, 5, 10]:
    80	    denom = {t for t, v in fl_in_truth.items() if v >= k}

codex
## Bottom Line

A “full-length” transcriptome alignment and a full genome intron chain are **different claims**. The transcriptome alignment says the read is sequence-compatible end-to-end with a linear reference transcript. It does not prove that minimap2 can place the same bases uniquely at the correct genomic locus, recognize every small terminal exon, or choose the reference splice coordinates.

Pyfin then makes the discrepancy absolute: every genome CIGAR `N` becomes an exact `(start, end)` intron (`fin/candidates/intron_chains.py:18`, `fin/candidates/intron_chains.py:35`), and `cluster_families` preserves those exact chains without rewriting them (`fin/candidates/chain_cluster.py:431`, `fin/candidates/chain_cluster.py:473`). Related chains share a family, but family membership does not create a new full or consensus chain. Therefore, if no genome-derived chain equals the truth tuple, no exact candidate exists.

## 1. Mechanism

### Likely ranking

1. **Dropped terminal exon/junction, including small terminal exons — dominant.**
2. **Junction-placement wobble — clearly secondary but material.**
3. **Primary genome alignment at another locus/strand — smaller, concentrated in D/E.**
4. **Fusion exclusion from long soft clips — probably smaller, but hidden inside the current categories.**
5. **True generation/emission bugs — consistent with the 1.7% A category.**

The 74%/10% split strongly says this is primarily an **alignment representation problem**, not clustering loss.

### A. Junction wobble

Transcriptome alignment has no genomic intron to place: the exon–exon sequence is contiguous in the target. Genome alignment must decide exactly where a noisy read changes from one exon to the next. Nearby repeats, indels, homopolymers, noncanonical sites, and equivalent local alignments can shift either boundary several bases.

Pyfin records the chosen coordinates literally; no tolerance exists at candidate equality (`fin/candidates/intron_chains.py:35`, `fin/candidates/discovery.py:32`). Wobble joins chains into the same family through `_wobble`, but still keeps both coordinate variants (`fin/candidates/chain_cluster.py:144`, `fin/candidates/chain_cluster.py:431`).

**Confirm it by read-name join, not merely locus scanning:**

- Collect the exact QNAMEs that give FL support in the transcriptome BAM.
- Find all genome primary/secondary/supplementary records for those QNAMEs.
- For equal-length genome and truth chains, tabulate donor and acceptor deltas separately.
- Check whether the error is random around truth or systematically shifted.
- Inspect splice motifs and minimap2 `cs`, `NM`, `AS`, `SA`, and `tp` tags directly with pysam; `alignment_to_dict` currently does not retain these tags (`fin/io/io_bam.py:203`).

A family modal coordinate can recover truth only when truth coordinates are actually represented—or are selected through extra genome-motif logic. If every genome read systematically chooses `truth+3`, pure modal consensus returns `truth+3`, not truth.

### B. Terminal clipping/dropped exons

This is the best explanation for most C cases. A transcriptome target lets a read align continuously across the exon junction. Against the genome, minimap2 may stop inside an internal exon, soft-clip the remaining query, split it into a supplementary alignment, or fail to represent a small terminal exon as a separate genomic block. `extract_intron_chain` consequently sees only the remaining `N` operations and returns a proper sub-chain.

Direct RNA’s 5′ truncation bias makes loss of the 5′-most exon/junction especially plausible, but the key observation here is stronger: some reads possess the sequence according to the transcriptome alignment, yet the genome spliced alignment does not encode it.

**Confirm per FL QNAME:**

- Compare transcriptome and genome `query_alignment_start/end`.
- Record terminal `S`/`H` lengths and which transcript end is missing.
- Verify whether the genome chain is a prefix, suffix, or internal sub-chain.
- Project the missing truth exon length and junction onto the clipped query segment.
- Locally align that clipped segment back to the same chromosome/locus and strand.
- Stratify C by missing terminal-exon length, number of missing junctions, soft-clip length, and whether an `SA` record covers the missing query bases.

A strong enrichment for one missing terminal intron plus a short terminal exon would directly support mechanisms B/E. Small terminal-exon loss is therefore mostly a **specific subtype of terminal clipping**, not an independent category.

### C. Primary alignment at a paralog or other locus

An end-to-end transcriptome hit may be non-unique among related transcripts. On the genome, minimap2 can choose a paralog, pseudogene, repeat-rich locus, or opposite-strand placement as primary.

There is an important code asymmetry:

- Interval construction discards secondary alignments (`fin/io/interval_manager.py:233`) and classifies supplementary alignments as fusion (`fin/io/interval_manager.py:243`).
- Intervals are separated by chromosome and BAM orientation (`fin/io/interval_manager.py:285`).
- Discovery filters fetched alignments to the interval’s strand (`fin/candidates/discovery.py:393`).
- But discovery itself does **not** reject secondary alignments before using their CIGAR chains; it only restricts sequence capture to primary records (`fin/candidates/discovery.py:406`).

Thus a truth-locus secondary alignment cannot create a de-novo interval, but may contribute structurally if another read created that interval. This makes behavior coverage-dependent.

**Confirm by QNAME:**

- Identify the genome primary locus, strand, MAPQ and score.
- Check whether the truth locus has a secondary or supplementary record.
- Compare primary/secondary alignment-score margins.
- Count cases where the truth-locus record exists but cannot create an interval.
- Distinguish “no genome alignment for this FL read” from “genome primary elsewhere.” Your current locus-wide E category does not fully make that distinction.

This mechanism should dominate true E cases and part of D, not most of C.

### D. Fusion exclusion

`is_fusion_read` excludes every supplementary alignment and every alignment with a terminal soft clip of at least 250 bp (`fin/io/interval_manager.py:135`, `fin/io/interval_manager.py:146`, `fin/io/interval_manager.py:149`, `fin/io/interval_manager.py:159`). Discovery applies the same exclusion before generation (`fin/candidates/discovery.py:413`).

That is a coarse rule: a 250 bp terminal clip can be a genuine fusion, but also a failed terminal-exon placement, adapter-rich sequence, or ordinary alignment failure.

Your diagnostic also excludes those alignments before classifying chains, so fusion-excluded reads can be hidden inside C/D/E rather than appearing as their own cause (`experiments/wobble_heya8/fl_missed_fast.py:128`, `experiments/wobble_heya8/fl_missed_fast.py:135`).

**Confirm by adding a pre-filter category:**

- For each FL QNAME, test `is_fusion_read` before discarding it.
- Remap the clipped sequence.
- Classify the result as collinear/same-locus, distant-locus, opposite-strand, or unmapped.
- A collinear same-locus placement is failed spliced alignment, not compelling fusion evidence.

## 2. Data-Only Recovery

### I. Read-consensus junctions

**Useful for part of the 10%; not guaranteed to recover exact truth.**

There is already a consensus implementation, but it pools donors and acceptors independently and rewrites read chains (`fin/candidates/intron_chains.py:193`, `fin/candidates/intron_chains.py:230`, `fin/candidates/intron_chains.py:262`). It additionally prefers canonical genome motifs (`fin/candidates/intron_chains.py:164`). That is not annotation snapping, but interval-wide independent donor/acceptor consensus can combine evidence from neighboring isoforms and create donor–acceptor pairings no read observed.

A safer design is:

- Operate within one read-derived family.
- Cluster complete junction pairs `(donor, acceptor)`, not endpoints independently.
- Select only an **observed** modal pair.
- Emit the consensus chain as an additional hypothesis.
- Keep every original exact variant unchanged.

This recovers truth when different reads wobble at different junctions and the truth coordinate is modal or at least observed. It cannot recover a systematically unobserved truth coordinate. Genome-motif-guided alternative generation could do that, but should remain a separately tagged hypothesis, not silently replace the read consensus.

### II. Path completion/intron graph

**This is the best data-only attack on the 74%, with a hard observability ceiling.**

The existing graph code already has the right conceptual machinery:

- Junctions are mapped to read-count centroids (`fin/candidates/junction_graph.py:29`).
- Edges exist only when two junctions are consecutive in an actual read (`fin/candidates/junction_graph.py:54`).
- Extension follows only unique supported predecessors/successors (`fin/candidates/junction_graph.py:106`, `fin/candidates/junction_graph.py:136`, `fin/candidates/junction_graph.py:149`).
- A TSS brake attempts to preserve genuine contained short isoforms (`fin/candidates/junction_graph.py:125`).

For example, reads carrying `[I1,I2]` and `[I2,I3]` provide phased edges `I1→I2` and `I2→I3`; an unbranched `[I1,I2,I3]` candidate can be assembled without annotation.

But it **cannot invent an entirely unobserved terminal junction**. If every genome alignment is the same nested 3′ suffix `[I2,I3]`, no graph can infer `I1`. Therefore, first compute the C-category recovery ceiling:

1. Does the union of genome-read junctions contain every truth junction within 6 bp?
2. Is every truth adjacency directly phased in at least one read?
3. Does the resulting path have no supported branch?
4. Would modal junction coordinates equal the truth chain?

The fraction satisfying all four is the maximum recoverable by a CIGAR-only graph.

The current experimental graph is not available through the production chain-cluster route: `chain_cluster` returns immediately (`fin/candidates/discovery.py:428`), while graph assembly occurs only in the legacy branch later (`fin/candidates/discovery.py:467`). That is the integration gap.

### III. Transcriptome BAM as evidence

**Good diagnostic oracle; inappropriate as the default de-novo generator when aligned to GENCODE.**

The transcriptome BAM directly imports reference-transcript structures and transcript identities. Using it to choose genomic intron chains would effectively make GENCODE an annotation guide, even if the operation is described as “second evidence.” It would also compromise the corrupted-annotation advantage and inherit transcriptome multi-mapping ambiguity.

Acceptable uses are:

- Measuring the FL-supported denominator, as currently done (`experiments/wobble_heya8/compute_fl.py:34`).
- Selecting read QNAMEs for diagnostics.
- An explicitly labeled guided/oracle rescue experiment.
- Possibly identifying which raw query segment remains unexplained, followed by **genome-local sequence realignment without using transcript coordinates**.

The production de-novo path should consume raw reads plus genome, not the GENCODE transcriptome alignment.

## 3. Avoiding Double Snap and Over-Merging

Use an **additive, provenance-preserving hypothesis model**:

- Never rewrite or delete the original exact CIGAR chains.
- Emit `observed`, `junction_consensus`, and `assembled_path` as separate source types.
- Derive consensus once from immutable raw primary-read junctions.
- Use complete junction pairs and directly observed consecutive-junction edges.
- Require at least two unique primary reads per graph edge; consider stronger support for a junction coordinate that differs from another well-supported coordinate.
- Stop at every supported branch; never choose the locally most abundant branch and continue.
- Do not permit single-linkage family bridges to create paths across unrelated endpoints merely because A is close to B and B is close to C.
- Preserve the exact read/edge provenance of every synthetic candidate.
- Put original and synthetic variants into the same pooled family, then let assignment select abundance; generation should not transfer ownership.
- Exempt synthetic candidates from any later coordinate snap/wobble rewrite. One raw-to-consensus mapping must be the only coordinate-normalization operation.
- Keep the feature reversible behind one flag and report how many candidates were observed versus synthesized.

This matches the existing `ChainFamily` intent: variants are preserved, reads are pooled at family level, and final ownership is deferred (`fin/candidates/chain_cluster.py:70`).

## 4. Recommendation

**Implement family-local, additive intron-graph hypothesis generation immediately after `cluster_families`.**

Concretely:

1. Start from each family’s raw variants and `variant_reads`.
2. Cluster complete observed junction pairs within 6 bp to an observed modal coordinate.
3. Build only directly read-phased adjacency edges.
4. Extend through edges supported by at least two unique primary reads and having exactly one predecessor/successor.
5. Add each resulting maximal chain as a new candidate while retaining all original variants.
6. Tag it as synthetic and preserve node/edge/read provenance.
7. Apply the strand-aware TSS brake to prevent extension over genuine contained isoforms.

This is the highest-leverage change because it targets the 74% dominant class and also normalizes recoverable wobble cases. It reuses the core logic already present in `junction_graph.py`, but places it in the production family workflow and changes its semantics from “rewrite/group reads by the extended chain” to “add a reversible structural hypothesis.”

### Measurement

Primary endpoint:

```text
FL3 exact recall =
  number of FL>=3 truth transcripts with a generated '=' chain
  -------------------------------------------------------------
  number of FL>=3 truth transcripts
```

Report alongside:

- Recovery separately for baseline B, C, D and E.
- CIGAR-graph recoverability ceiling for category C.
- Raw generation precision: exact truth-matching chains / all generated chains.
- Final post-assignment precision and recall.
- Number of added consensus and assembled candidates.
- False merges among genuine close splice-site isoforms.
- Junctions and edges supported by one, two, or three-plus reads.
- Recovery attributable exclusively to synthetic candidates.

A successful result should produce a large C-category recall gain, some B gain, and only a small candidate-count/precision penalty. If the recoverability ceiling shows that most C transcripts lack any genome-CIGAR evidence for the missing terminal junction, the next intervention should be local spliced realignment of terminal clipped query sequence—not transcriptome-guided rescue.

No code was modified.
