This mechanism should dominate true E cases and part of D, not most of C.

### D. Fusion exclusion

`is_fusion_read` excludes every supplementary alignment and every alignment with a terminal soft clip of at least 250 bp (`fin/io/interval_manager.py:135`, `fin/io/interval_manager.py:146`, `fin/io/interval_manager.py:149`, `fin/io/interval_manager.py:159`). Discovery applies the same exclusion before generation (`fin/candidates/discovery.py:413`).

That is a coarse rule: a 250 bp terminal clip can be a genuine fusion, but also a failed terminal-exon placement, adapter-rich sequence, or ordinary alignment failure.

Your diagnostic also excludes those alignments before classifying chains, so fusion-excluded reads can be hidden inside C/D/E rather than appearing as their own cause (`experiments/wobble_heya8/fl_missed_fast.py:128`, `experiments/wobble_heya8/fl_missed_fast.py:135`).

**Confirm by adding a pre-filter category:**

- For each FL QNAME, test `is_fusion_read` before discarding it.
- Remap the clipped sequence.
- Classify the result as collinear/same-locus, distant-locus, opposite-strand, or unmapped.
- A collinear same-locus placement is failed spliced alignment, not compelling fusion evidence.

## 2. Data-Only Recovery

### I. Read-consensus junctions

**Useful for part of the 10%; not guaranteed to recover exact truth.**

There is already a consensus implementation, but it pools donors and acceptors independently and rewrites read chains (`fin/candidates/intron_chains.py:193`, `fin/candidates/intron_chains.py:230`, `fin/candidates/intron_chains.py:262`). It additionally prefers canonical genome motifs (`fin/candidates/intron_chains.py:164`). That is not annotation snapping, but interval-wide independent donor/acceptor consensus can combine evidence from neighboring isoforms and create donor–acceptor pairings no read observed.

A safer design is:

- Operate within one read-derived family.
- Cluster complete junction pairs `(donor, acceptor)`, not endpoints independently.
- Select only an **observed** modal pair.
- Emit the consensus chain as an additional hypothesis.
- Keep every original exact variant unchanged.

This recovers truth when different reads wobble at different junctions and the truth coordinate is modal or at least observed. It cannot recover a systematically unobserved truth coordinate. Genome-motif-guided alternative generation could do that, but should remain a separately tagged hypothesis, not silently replace the read consensus.

### II. Path completion/intron graph

**This is the best data-only attack on the 74%, with a hard observability ceiling.**

The existing graph code already has the right conceptual machinery:

- Junctions are mapped to read-count centroids (`fin/candidates/junction_graph.py:29`).
- Edges exist only when two junctions are consecutive in an actual read (`fin/candidates/junction_graph.py:54`).
- Extension follows only unique supported predecessors/successors (`fin/candidates/junction_graph.py:106`, `fin/candidates/junction_graph.py:136`, `fin/candidates/junction_graph.py:149`).
- A TSS brake attempts to preserve genuine contained short isoforms (`fin/candidates/junction_graph.py:125`).

For example, reads carrying `[I1,I2]` and `[I2,I3]` provide phased edges `I1→I2` and `I2→I3`; an unbranched `[I1,I2,I3]` candidate can be assembled without annotation.

But it **cannot invent an entirely unobserved terminal junction**. If every genome alignment is the same nested 3′ suffix `[I2,I3]`, no graph can infer `I1`. Therefore, first compute the C-category recovery ceiling:

1. Does the union of genome-read junctions contain every truth junction within 6 bp?
2. Is every truth adjacency directly phased in at least one read?
3. Does the resulting path have no supported branch?
4. Would modal junction coordinates equal the truth chain?

The fraction satisfying all four is the maximum recoverable by a CIGAR-only graph.

The current experimental graph is not available through the production chain-cluster route: `chain_cluster` returns immediately (`fin/candidates/discovery.py:428`), while graph assembly occurs only in the legacy branch later (`fin/candidates/discovery.py:467`). That is the integration gap.

### III. Transcriptome BAM as evidence

**Good diagnostic oracle; inappropriate as the default de-novo generator when aligned to GENCODE.**

The transcriptome BAM directly imports reference-transcript structures and transcript identities. Using it to choose genomic intron chains would effectively make GENCODE an annotation guide, even if the operation is described as “second evidence.” It would also compromise the corrupted-annotation advantage and inherit transcriptome multi-mapping ambiguity.

Acceptable uses are:

- Measuring the FL-supported denominator, as currently done (`experiments/wobble_heya8/compute_fl.py:34`).
- Selecting read QNAMEs for diagnostics.
- An explicitly labeled guided/oracle rescue experiment.
- Possibly identifying which raw query segment remains unexplained, followed by **genome-local sequence realignment without using transcript coordinates**.

The production de-novo path should consume raw reads plus genome, not the GENCODE transcriptome alignment.

## 3. Avoiding Double Snap and Over-Merging

Use an **additive, provenance-preserving hypothesis model**:

- Never rewrite or delete the original exact CIGAR chains.
- Emit `observed`, `junction_consensus`, and `assembled_path` as separate source types.
- Derive consensus once from immutable raw primary-read junctions.
- Use complete junction pairs and directly observed consecutive-junction edges.
- Require at least two unique primary reads per graph edge; consider stronger support for a junction coordinate that differs from another well-supported coordinate.
- Stop at every supported branch; never choose the locally most abundant branch and continue.
- Do not permit single-linkage family bridges to create paths across unrelated endpoints merely because A is close to B and B is close to C.
- Preserve the exact read/edge provenance of every synthetic candidate.
- Put original and synthetic variants into the same pooled family, then let assignment select abundance; generation should not transfer ownership.
- Exempt synthetic candidates from any later coordinate snap/wobble rewrite. One raw-to-consensus mapping must be the only coordinate-normalization operation.
- Keep the feature reversible behind one flag and report how many candidates were observed versus synthesized.

This matches the existing `ChainFamily` intent: variants are preserved, reads are pooled at family level, and final ownership is deferred (`fin/candidates/chain_cluster.py:70`).

## 4. Recommendation

**Implement family-local, additive intron-graph hypothesis generation immediately after `cluster_families`.**

Concretely:

1. Start from each family’s raw variants and `variant_reads`.
2. Cluster complete observed junction pairs within 6 bp to an observed modal coordinate.
3. Build only directly read-phased adjacency edges.
4. Extend through edges supported by at least two unique primary reads and having exactly one predecessor/successor.
5. Add each resulting maximal chain as a new candidate while retaining all original variants.
6. Tag it as synthetic and preserve node/edge/read provenance.
7. Apply the strand-aware TSS brake to prevent extension over genuine contained isoforms.

This is the highest-leverage change because it targets the 74% dominant class and also normalizes recoverable wobble cases. It reuses the core logic already present in `junction_graph.py`, but places it in the production family workflow and changes its semantics from “rewrite/group reads by the extended chain” to “add a reversible structural hypothesis.”

### Measurement

Primary endpoint:

```text
FL3 exact recall =
  number of FL>=3 truth transcripts with a generated '=' chain
  -------------------------------------------------------------
  number of FL>=3 truth transcripts
```

Report alongside:

- Recovery separately for baseline B, C, D and E.
- CIGAR-graph recoverability ceiling for category C.
- Raw generation precision: exact truth-matching chains / all generated chains.
- Final post-assignment precision and recall.
- Number of added consensus and assembled candidates.
- False merges among genuine close splice-site isoforms.
- Junctions and edges supported by one, two, or three-plus reads.
- Recovery attributable exclusively to synthetic candidates.

A successful result should produce a large C-category recall gain, some B gain, and only a small candidate-count/precision penalty. If the recoverability ceiling shows that most C transcripts lack any genome-CIGAR evidence for the missing terminal junction, the next intervention should be local spliced realignment of terminal clipped query sequence—not transcriptome-guided rescue.

No code was modified.
